L21: ## 1. Executive Summary
L22: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L23: Ublituximab-xiiy (TG1101) is a recombinant IgG1 chimeric, glycoengineered monoclonal antibody targeting the CD20 antigen, leading to lysis of B-cells. Ublituximab-xiiy targets a novel epitope of CD20 that is not bound by ofatumumab or ocrelizumab. (b) (4)
L24: and therefore result in greater antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) than rituximab and ocrelizumab.
L25: Two anti-CD20 monoclonal antibodies, ocrelizumab (Ocrevus) and ofatumumab (Kesimpta), are approved for the treatment of relapsing forms of multiple sclerosis (RMS) in adults. Ocrelizumab is also approved for the treatment of primary progressive multiple sclerosis. The precise mechanism by which anti-CD20 therapies exert therapeutic effects in multiple sclerosis is unknown, but is presumed to involve binding to CD20 on B-lymphocytes, which leads to cytolysis via ADCC and/or complement-mediated lysis.
L26: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L27: Two adequate and well-controlled, identical Phase 3 trials provided substantial evidence that treatment with ublituximab-xiiy administered intravenously (IV) at an initial dose of 150mg, then 450mg on Day 15, and 450mg every 6 months thereafter, reduces the frequency of relapses in comparison to treatment with teriflunomide in patients with relapsing forms of MS. Treatment with ublituximab-xiiy significantly reduced the frequency of new MRI lesions (both gadolinium-enhancing and new/enlarging T2 lesions) in comparison to teriflunomide. However, ublituximab-xiiy did not demonstrate benefit compared to teriflunomide in terms of 3-month confirmed disability progression.
L28: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L29: ## Benefit-Risk Integrated Assessment
L30: Two adequate and well-controlled, identical Phase 3 trials in adults with RMS provided substantial evidence that treatment with ublituximab-xiiy significantly reduces annualized relapse rate and the frequency of new MRI lesions (both gadolinium-enhancing and new/enlarging T2 lesions) in comparison to teriflunomide. However, ublituximab-xiiy did not demonstrate benefit compared to teriflunomide in terms of 12-week confirmed disability progression. These findings represent clinically relevant benefits that justify a moderate safety risk, similar to other anti-CD20 therapies approved for RMS. The risks associated with ublituximab-xiiy treatment were consistent with those observed with other anti-CD20 therapies, including infections (including serious and fatal infections), infusion related reactions, reduction in IgM, cytopenias, and reduction in immunoglobulins (particularly IgM). Opportunistic infections and reduction in IgG were not observed in controlled RMS trials of ublituximab-xiiy; however, longer-term experience is needed to fully assess these risks. Therefore, based on available data, the risk profile appears manageable and consistent with other anti-CD20 therapies, and justifies the benefit of treatment with ublituximab-xiiy in patients with RMS.
L31: ## Benefit-Risk Dimensions
L32: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Multiple sclerosis (MS) is a chronic, immune-mediated, demyelinating, and neurodegenerative disease affecting the central nervous system.
Relapsing forms of MS are defined by the occurrence of clinical relapses, which are events characterized by new onset neurological symptoms associated with disability, from which there is variable recovery. Relapsing forms of MS include clinically isolated syndrome, relapsing remitting MS, and active secondary progressive MS.
The prevention of relapses is paramount to the treatment of patients with relapsing forms of MS, in order to prevent new neurological symptoms that can lead to disability.
Treatment of relapsing forms of multiple sclerosis is intended to reduce the frequency of clinical relapses. Clinical relapses can be permanently disabling and reduce quality of life.
L33: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Current Treatment Options	There are over twenty products approved for treatment of relapsing forms of MS.
Currently approved MS therapeutics have demonstrated efficacy in reducing the risk of clinical relapse, which is presumed to be related to immunomodulatory mechanisms of action that impact the neuroinflammatory aspect of the disease.
Treatment of relapsing forms of multiple sclerosis generally involves immunomodulation intended to reduce the frequency of clinical relapse. Many therapies approved for the treatment of relapsing forms of MS are labeled to prevent accumulation of disability.
Benefit	Two adequate and well-controlled, identical Phase 3 trials in adults with RMS provided substantial evidence that treatment with ublituximab-xiiy significantly reduces annualized relapse rate and the frequency of new MRI lesions (both gadolinium-enhancing and new/enlarging T2 lesions) in comparison to teriflunomide.
Ublituximab-xiiy did not demonstrate benefit compared to teriflunomide in terms of 12-week confirmed disability progression.
There is substantial evidence that treatment of RMS with ublituximab-xiiy provides a clinically meaningful benefit (i.e., reduction of relapses).
Risk and Risk Management	A major risk of ublituximab-xiiy is that of infection. Serious and fatal infections occurred in ublituximab-xiiy-treated patients during the controlled trials. Serious infections also have been reported with other anti-CD20 therapies approved for RMS, and the relative risk compared to teriflunomide appears similar to that observed in controlled trials for ublituximab-xiiy.
Ongoing clinical surveillance and monitoring of immunoglobulins may be able to mitigate the risk of more serious infections.
Infusion-related reactions, including some serious reactions, were observed. Patients will be required to receive premedication with corticosteroids and an antihistamine, and remain under observation post-infusion.
The risk of treatment of RMS with ublituximab-xiiy appears consistent with that of other anti-CD20 therapies approved for RMS. Infusion related reactions appear manageable with required premedication and post-infusion monitoring. The demonstrated benefit appears to justify the risks.

L37: ## 2. Therapeutic Context
L38: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L39: Multiple sclerosis (MS) is a chronic, immune-mediated, demyelinating, and neurodegenerative disorder affecting the central nervous system. The cause of MS is unknown, but is hypothesized to be related to a complex interaction of genetic and environmental risk factors.1,2 MS is the most common cause of non-traumatic neurologic disability in young adults, and it is estimated that approximately 1 million people in the United States have MS.3 Disease manifestations can be diverse, and include weakness, incoordination, visual impairment, sensory loss, gait dysfunction, fatigue, cognitive impairment, and bowel/bladder dysfunction.
L40: Relapsing forms of MS are defined by the occurrence of clinical relapses, which are events characterized by new onset neurological symptoms associated with disability, from which there is variable recovery. Relapsing forms of MS include clinically isolated syndrome, relapsing remitting MS (RRMS), and active secondary progressive MS. The prevention of relapses is paramount to the treatment of patients with relapsing forms of MS; relapses degrade the quality of life in patients with MS, and prevention of relapses prevents new neurological symptoms that can lead to disability.
L41: Relapsing-remitting MS is the most common MS phenotype, as it accounts for approximately 85% of MS cases at symptom onset.4 Patients with RRMS present with clinical relapses, from which there is a spectrum of recovery. The median age of RRMS onset is approximately 28 to 30 years.5-7 In general, the frequency of relapse decreases over time,8,9 with an estimate of relapse decrease by 17% for every 5 years of disease duration.8 The decrease in relapse risk is thought to be more pronounced in patients with older age of onset.
L42: Over time, the majority of patients with RRMS transition from predominantly inflammatory to more neurodegenerative driving forces of the disease, with development of secondary progressive MS (SPMS).10 The transition to SPMS is an age-dependent process,11-13 and it is estimated that approximately 30 to 40% of patients with SPMS have relapses superimposed upon this typical gradual decline.6,14 The time from RRMS diagnosis to SPMS conversion is age of onset-dependent, and ranges from 18.9 to 20 years for all patients per several natural history studies.8,12,15,16 Generally, the younger a patient is at the time of RRMS onset, the longer the disease duration prior to SPMS conversion. However, several studies have demonstrated that patients who receive MS treatments have substantially decreased risk of and delayed conversion to SPMS compared to patients in the pre-treatment era.17-23 Additionally, use of some therapies approved to treat MS has been shown to delay reaching disability milestones.17,20-22
L43: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L44: There are over 20 products approved for treatment of relapsing forms of MS, including clinically isolated syndrome, relapsing remitting MS, and active secondary progressive MS (Table 1). Currently approved MS therapeutics have demonstrated efficacy in reducing the risk of clinical relapse, which is presumed to be related to immunomodulatory mechanisms of action that impact the neuroinflammatory aspect of the disease.
L45: Table 1 (Reviewer). FDA-Approved Treatments for Relapsing Forms of Multiple Sclerosis

L52: ## 3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L53: Ublituximab-xiiy is not approved for any indication in the United States. IND (b) (4) for ublituximab for the treatment of (b) (4), along with IND 116762 for umbralisib, was placed on partial hold on 1/13/2022 due to concerns regarding mortality, serious adverse events, and questionable benefit of the combination of ublituximab and umbralisib. Under this partial hold, no new subjects were to be enrolled in the specified trials, and current subjects were required to be reconsented.
L54: BLA 761207 for ublituximab and umbralisib for the treatment of chronic lymphocytic leukemia was withdrawn by the applicant prior to a planned Advisory Committee that was convened due to a mortality signal believed to be associated with umbralisib.

L67: Of note, the Division had several concerns related to safety and safety reporting in the context of the ublituximab-xiiy IND. Serious infections and deaths were reported in the RMS development program in 2019, which prompted the Division to request that the applicant update the informed consent documents and Investigator's Brochure, reconsent subjects, and inform investigators of the risk of life-threatening and serious infections on 12/18/2019.
L68: Subsequently, the Division raised concerns due to the delayed reporting of a death in the ublituximab-xiiy development program in February of 2020, which prompted a For Cause inspection by the Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) in January of 2021. This inspection did not result in any citations. However, the inspection found that the applicant did not have a formal standard operating procedure for assessing safety during the Phase 3 trials, and that a rationale for assessing relatedness to study treatment was unclear (which was also noted by the Data Safety Monitoring Board).
L69: The IND annual report submitted 7/21/2020 indicated the death of a 22 year-old woman due to salpingitis, but no safety report was submitted to the IND. A letter was sent to the applicant on 8/25/2020 regarding IND safety reporting requirements, including the submission of safety reports for all serious infections and deaths, regardless of the applicant's causality determination.
L70: The applicant identified hepatitis B reactivation as an important potential risk of ublituximab-xiiy, and sent a Dear Investigator Letter regarding this risk dated 11/2/2020, which was submitted to the Division on 11/3/2020. The applicant revised this letter following Division feedback, then resubmitted to the Division on 11/20/2020. This revision indicated that "investigators should consider rescreeing any subjects with RMS deemed to be at high risk of hepatitis B infection."

L95: ## 5.2. Review Strategy
L96: The efficacy portion of this review was focused on two pivotal trials, TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. These double-blind trials were of identical design, conducted under Special Protocol Agreements, and utilized bioequivalent teriflunomide as a comparator. The treatment duration of 96 weeks, with 120-week follow-up, is adequate to support an indication for both reduction in annualized relapse rate (ARR), the primary endpoint, and 12- and 24-week confirmed disability progression. These trials will be analyzed separately for their primary endpoint, ARR, and pooled for analysis of 12- and 24-week confirmed disability progression, which was an *a priori* decision. The results of Study TG1101-RMS201 will be discussed as supportive evidence of efficacy.
L97: The safety portion of this review will focus on the double-blind portion of the Phase 3 Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, as well as their open-label extension (TG1101-RMS303). The results of Study TG1101-RMS201 and its open label-extension, TG1101-RMS201E, will be discussed as well. The applicant's Integrated Summary of Safety was separated into Phase 2 and Phase 3 datasets, as previously agreed upon by the Division. Please refer to Section 8.1 for further details regarding the safety review strategy.
L98: Dr. Rui Li, Senior Clinical Analyst, performed several of the reported safety analyses in conjunction with this reviewer.
L99: ## 6. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L100: ## 6.1. Study TG1101-RMS301
L101: ## 6.1.1. Study Design
L102: ## Overview and Objective
L103: Study TG1101-RMS301 ("ULTIMATE I") was one of two identical randomized controlled trials (RCTs) whose objective was to assess the efficacy and safety of ublituximab-xiiy compared to bioequivalent teriflunomide as treatment for relapsing forms of MS. Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 were conducted under a Special Protocol Agreement.
L104: The primary objective of the study was to determine the Annualized Relapse Rate (ARR) in subjects with RMS after 96 weeks (approximately 2 years with a year equal to 365.25 days) treatment with IV infusion of ublituximab-xiiy/oral placebo compared to 14 mg oral teriflunomide/IV placebo.
L105: Secondary objectives were to examine the effects of ublituximab-xiiy/oral placebo as compared to teriflunomide/IV placebo on the following:
L106: - MRI parameters,
- Confirmed Disability Progression,
- No Evidence of Disease Activity (NEDA),
- Symbol Digit Modality Test (SDMT), and
- Safety of ublituximab-xiiy/oral placebo, as determined by adverse events (AEs) and serious adverse event (SAEs), including MS worsening.
L107: ## Trial Design
L108: Study TG1101-RMS301 was a 120-week, Phase 3, randomized, multicenter, double-blind, double-dummy, active controlled study, designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of ublituximab-xiiy compared to bioequivalent teriflunomide in patients with relapsing forms of MS per 2010 McDonald Criteria. Patients were required to have at least 2 relapses in the prior 2 years, or 1 relapse in the prior year and/or at least 1 gadolinium-enhancing lesion. Patients were randomized 1:1 to receive treatment with either ublituximab-xiiy or teriflunomide (with oral or IV placebo, respectively), for 96 weeks (last ublituximab-xiiy infusion at Week 72). Double-blind period follow-up continued until 120 weeks. Upon completion of the double-blind treatment period, patients could consent to participation in the 172-week open label extension study, TG1101-RMS303.
L109: The study schematic for Study TG1101-RMS301 is presented in Figure 1. The study consisted of a Screening Period of up to 4 weeks, a 96-week Double-blind Treatment Period, and a Follow-Up Period of 20 weeks.
L110: Figure 1 (Applicant). Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 Study Schematic
L111: Source: Applicant's Clinical Study Report, Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302
L112: ## Blinding
L113: Study TG1101-RMS301 utilized a double-dummy design, where patients assigned ublituximab-xiiy received oral placebo, and patients assigned teriflunomide received IV placebo. Teriflunomide and oral placebo were identical in appearance, and were supplied in identical boxes. Ublituximab-xiiy and IV placebo were provided in unit boxes containing either 15mL or 6mL vials, respectively, to the study site.
L114: The Independent Relapse Adjudication Panel (IRAP), which was responsible for confirmation of relapses to be included in the primary efficacy analysis, was blinded. Study staff, including the examining and treating neurologists, were blinded to treatment assignment. Additionally, the examining neurologist was blinded to the treating neurologist's assessments, and vice-versa. MRIs were reviewed by a centralized reading center blinded to treatment assignment and any clinical information.
L115: The protocol also incorporated a Blinded Assessment Relapse Team (BART) that reassessed sample size when 210 out of the 220 participants in each arm were randomized. The Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) reviewed unblinded safety data.

Laura Baldassari, MD, MHS
BLA 761238
Ublituximab-xiiy (Briumvi)

L119: ## *Key Inclusion Criteria*
L120: - Age 18 to 55 years
- Diagnosis of relapsing MS per 2010 McDonald Criteria
- \ge 2 relapses in prior 2 years or 1 relapse in the year prior to screening and/or \ge 1 Gd enhancing lesion
- Documented MRI of brain with abnormalities consistent with MS
- Active disease
- EDSS 0-5.5 (inclusive) at screening
- B cell counts \ge 5\% of total lymphocytes
- Neurologic stability \ge 30 days prior to screening and baseline

L122: - Prior treatment with any of the following:Anti-CD20 or other B-cell directed treatment

- Natalizumab
- Teriflunomide
- Leflunomide

- 6 months with daclizumab, azathioprine, methotrexate, or cyclophosphamide
- 90 days with fingolimod, or experimental S1P modulators, IV immunoglobulin, and plasmapheresis
- 30 days with glatiramer acetate, interferons, dimethyl fumarate, laquinimod or glucocorticoids
- Diagnosis of primary progressive MS
- Disease duration \ge 10 years from onset with EDSS \le 2.0
- Contraindications to MRI, gadolinium, or teriflunomide
- Evidence or known history of clinically significant infection (e.g., PML, tuberculosis, hepatitis C, hepatitis B, HIV/AIDS)
- History of liver disease
- History of renal impairment

c. Platelets &lt;150,000 cells/\text{mm}^3
d. Absolute neutrophil count \le 1,500 cells/\text{mm}^3

L125: ## *Dose Regimen*
L126: Subjects were randomized to receive either ublituximab-xiiy/PO placebo or teriflunomide 14mg daily/IV placebo for a 96-week double-blind treatment period. The dosing regimen, including ublituximab-xiiy dose and infusion times, are presented in Table 3.

L129: Source: Study TG1101-RMS301 Protocol Version 5.0 (9/4/2020)
L130: All subjects were required to undergo an accelerated elimination procedure at Week 100 or at the time of early treatment withdrawal. Plasma concentration of teriflunomide was measured in all subjects prior to (Week 96) and following (Week 116) the accelerated elimination procedure to assess compliance and maintain the study blind.
L131: ## *Management of Infusion-Related Reactions*
L132: Due to the known risk of infusion-related reactions, subjects received pre-medication 30 to 60 minutes prior to each study infusion with an antihistamine (diphenhydramine 50 mg or equivalent; administered orally), and a corticosteroid (dexamethasone 10-20 mg or equivalent; administered orally). Subjects could also receive prochlorperazine (or another antiemetic agents at Treating Neurologist/PI discretion if inadequate) for prevention or treatment of nausea/vomiting.
L133: The protocol indicates that symptomatic infusion reactions may be treated at the discretion of the treating physician, including but not limited to oral acetaminophen 650mg, corticosteroids, antihistamines, oxygen, and bronchodilators. Due to the potential for infusion-related hypotension, the protocol instructs investigators to consider withholding anti-hypertensive medications for 24 hours prior to study infusion.
L134: ## Relapse Assessment and Treatment
L135: Subjects who experienced new or worsening neurologic symptoms were instructed to contact the Treating Neurologist within 48 hours of symptom onset. Within 7 days of the subject reporting the suspected relapse to the Treating Neurologist, the subject will be scheduled for a study visit and assessed by both the Treating and Examining Neurologist independently. Relapse assessments included neurological and physical examination, concomitant medications, safety assessment, and Expanded Disability Status Scale (EDSS, by the examining neurologist). The treating neurologist will be blinded to the examining neurologist's EDSS assessment. After the completion of the examining neurologist's assessment, the treating neurologist may initiate relapse treatment with IV methylprednisolone 1g daily for 3 to 5 days.
L136: The results of the treating and examining neurologists' assessments were then sent to the Independent Relapse Adjudication Panel (IRAP) for confirmation. Relapses were confirmed if a subject experienced either a \ge 2 point increase on one or 1 point increase on 2 or more of the appropriate Functional Systems (which correspond to the patient's symptoms), or a \ge 0.5 point increase in EDSS (unless EDSS was 0, which requires an increase of at least 1 point) from the previous assessment. Additionally, relapses are defined in the protocol as "new or worsening neurological symptoms lasting \ge 24 hours with the absence of fever, injury, infection or adverse reactions to medications and accompanied by new neurological findings upon examination by the Examining Neurologist." Relapses were also to be preceded by neurological stability for at least 30 days.
L137: The protocol required all subjects who experience an IRAP-confirmed or Treating Neurologist medically-confirmed relapse to be reconsented within 6 weeks to continue trial participation.

L144: ## Disability
L145: Time to 12-week confirmed disability progression (CDP) was a key secondary endpoint for Study TG1101-RMS301, and was evaluated utilizing EDSS assessments performed by the Examining Neurologist at screening, Day 1, Weeks 12, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72, 84, and 96. Disability progression was defined as an increase of \ge 1.0 point from the baseline EDSS, not attributable to another etiology, when the baseline EDSS was \le 5.5, and \ge 0.5 when the baseline EDSS was &gt; 5.5. Confirmation of disability progression occurs when this increase in EDSS is recorded at the subsequent regularly scheduled study visit.
L146: ## Brain MRI
L147: Brain MRIs were obtained with and without gadolinium contrast in all patients at screening, Weeks 12, 24, 48, 96, and at early withdrawal (if an MRI was not obtained within 30 days). MRIs were evaluated locally for safety reasons, and were formally read by a centralized, blinded reading center for efficacy endpoints. MRI reports provided to the Treating Neurologist only indicated non-MS pathology.
L148: ## Study Endpoints
L149: The primary efficacy endpoint was ARR, defined as the number of IRAP-confirmed relapses per subject year. The estimate of ARR for a treatment group was the total number of relapses for subjects in the respective treatment group divided by the sum of treatment duration for subjects in that specific treatment group. Subjects were treated up to 96 weeks.
L150: Key secondary efficacy endpoints were:
L151: - Total number of gadolinium-enhancing T1-lesions per MRI scan by Week 96.
- Total number of new and enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per MRI scan by Week 96.
- Time to Confirmed Disability Progression (CDP) for at least 12 weeks occurring during the 96-week double-blind treatment period. This analysis utilized pooled data from the two identical studies, TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.
- Proportion of subjects with No Evidence of Disease Activity (NEDA) from Week 24 to Week 96.
- Proportion of subjects reaching impaired Symbol Digit Modalities Test (SDMT) from baseline to Week 96.
- Percentage change in Brain Volume from baseline to Week 96.
L152: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L153: ## Analysis Population
L154: The Intention-to-Treat (ITT) population was defined as all randomized subjects, analyzed by
L155: randomized treatment group. The ITT population was only utilized for sensitivity analyses of key efficacy endpoints.
L156: The modified Intention-to-Treat (mITT) population was defined as all subjects in the ITT population who received at least one dose of study medication and had at least one baseline and post-baseline efficacy assessment. The primary efficacy analyses were conducted in the mITT population.
L157: The Per-Protocol population was defined as all subjects in the mITT population who were treated for at least 1.75 years and did not have a major protocol deviation that impacted efficacy analysis.
L158: The Safety Population included all subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. All safety analyses were conducted in the Safety Population.
L159: ## Primary Endpoint
L160: The primary efficacy endpoint, ARR, was defined as the number of relapses (protocol-defined and IRAP-confirmed) per subject-year (with a year equal to 365.25 days) in the mITT population. This analysis was conducted using a negative binomial regression model to account for potential overdispersion, adjusted for EDSS stratum (baseline EDSS \le 3.5 versus &gt;3.5) and clinic region. The model evaluated the total number of confirmed relapses with onset between the date of randomization and the day of last study treatment, by treatment group. Log-transformed standardized treatment duration (randomization to date of last treatment before early withdrawal or completion of Week 96 assessment) was included in the model as an offset variable. This strategy resulted in a rate ratio with 95% confidence intervals to facilitate comparison of ARR between ublituximab-xiiy and teriflunomide.
L161: ## Key Secondary Endpoints
L162: Key secondary endpoints, listed above in order, were analyzed in a hierarchical approach using a step-down procedure. Again, the mITT population was utilized for each analysis.
L163: The total numbers of gadolinium-enhancing T1-lesions and new or enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per MRI scan were calculated as the sum of the number of lesions at Weeks 12, 24, 48, and 96, divided by the number of MRIs performed. These count data were analyzed using negative binomial regression, with offset based on time on study, and covariates of region, baseline EDSS stratum, and baseline MRI lesion counts.
L164: As mentioned above, time to 12-week CDP analysis utilized pooled data from Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. The applicant also analyzed time to 24-week CDP, as well as 12-
L165: Clinical Review
Laura Baldassari, MD, MHS
BLA 761238
Ublituximab-xiiy (Briumvi)
L166: and 24-week confirmed disability improvement (CDI) as tertiary analyses. Time to event analyses were conducted using Kaplan-Meier methods, which reported a median time to event with 95% confidence intervals.
L167: NEDA was defined as having no IRAP-confirmed relapses, MRI activity (no gadolinium-enhancing T1 or new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions), or 12-week CDP. The proportion of subjects with NEDA between Weeks 24 and 96 was compared using logistic regression, adjusted for region, baseline EDSS stratum, and baseline MRI lesion counts.
L168: Impairment on SDMT was defined as a decrease from baseline of at least 4 points on any post-baseline assessment, up to the Week 96 visit. The proportion of subjects who experienced impaired SDMT was compared using logistic regression, adjusted for region, baseline EDSS stratum, and log-transformed baseline MRI lesion counts.
L169: Percent brain volume change comparing ublituximab-xiiy- and teriflunomide-treated subjects was analyzed using linear mixed effects models with covariates region, baseline EDSS stratum, and baseline brain volume.

L179: ## 6.1.2. Study Results
L180: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L181: The Clinical Study Report submitted by the applicant states that the study was conducted in compliance with Good Clinical Practice guidelines, and in accordance with the protocol and "consensus ethical principles originating or derived from the Declaration of Helsinki."
L182: ## Financial Disclosure
L183: The applicant provided Form 3454 indicating that there were no financial arrangements with investigators whereby the value of compensation could be affected by the outcome of the study as defined in 21 CFR 54.2(a). No investigators had significant disclosed interest or were missing disclosure information for Study TG1101-RMS301. See Section 13.3.
L184: ## Patient Disposition
L185: In Study TG1101-RMS301, 646 subjects were screened, 97 were screen failures, and 549 were enrolled and randomized. The ITT population included 549 subjects (274 ublituximab-xiiy, 275 teriflunomide), the mITT population included 545 subjects (271 ublituximab-xiiy, 274 teriflunomide). One subject was randomized to the ublituximab-xiiy group but discontinued the study due to withdrawal of consent in the setting of difficult IV placement prior to receiving treatment.
L186: Overall, 492 (89.6%) subjects completed the 96-week double blind treatment period (240 [87.6%] ublituximab-xiiy, 252 [91.6%] teriflunomide), and 437 (79.6%) subjects completed the follow-up period through Week 120 (219 [79.9%] ublituximab-xiiy, 218 [79.3%] teriflunomide). Reasons for premature study drug discontinuation in the ITT population are delineated by treatment group in Table 6.
L187: *Reviewer comment: Premature study discontinuations due to adverse events were more frequent in the ublituximab-xiiy group compared to the teriflunomide group (6.2% versus 0.4%, respectively).*
L188: The safety population included 548 treated subjects, 273 who received ublituximab-xiiy and 275 who received teriflunomide.

L199: Reviewer comment: The number of major protocol violations that occurred is concerning, but the frequency appeared similar in both treatment groups. Potential unblinding events most commonly involved the treating neurologist being unblinded to MRI or EDSS. Please refer to the OSI Review. The safety laboratories not performed were most frequently pregnancy testing.
L200: One subject (Subject ID (b)(6)) experienced a major protocol deviation in which the ECRF/IXRS was not utilized for randomization, and the study nurse chose the treatment assignment as ublituximab-xiiy. The subject received ublituximab-xiiy on Week 1 Day 1, the PI identified the error, and the subject was randomized to teriflunomide, which was started on Week 1 Day 2. An Information Request was sent to the applicant on 2/10/22 regarding this
L201: deviation due to its serious nature, and the applicant responded on 3/4/22.
L202: Per the applicant, this subject was the first randomized at this site, and despite training, the study nurse "unintentionally circumvented the study centralized randomization process...and manually dispensed one ublituximab-xiiy/placebo kit...and one teriflunomide/placebo kit." This subject was infused with the ublituximab-xiiy/placebo kit and sent home with the teriflunomide/placebo kit. The next day, the site "discovered the subject had not been randomized in IWRS (Rave) and immediately contacted the subject...The subject reported taking 2 tablets of teriflunomide/placebo from the kit. The applicant was notified, the site was immediately re-trained...and the site was instructed to randomize the subject into IWRS (Rave) the same day." Following discussion, the applicant and medical monitor decided that the subject be discontinued from the study after the Week 2 Day 8 visit, and this subject is not included in the per protocol population.
L203: The applicant's investigation indicated that the root cause of this deviation was "the study nurse's lack of prior experience or sufficient familiarity with the centralized randomization process." The applicant took corrective and preventative actions, including retraining of staff at this study site and on-site assistance with the site's second randomization. The applicant indicated that no additional deviations related to randomization procedures occurred at this site, and that no other protocol deviations related to circumventing centralized randomization occurred in Studies TG1101-RMS301 or TG1101-RMS302.
L204: *Reviewer comment: This protocol deviation involved study staff circumventing the centralized randomization mechanism and dispensing both ublituximab-xiiy and teriflunomide kits, which is extremely concerning. However, it appears to be an isolated incident per the applicant's response to Information Request.*

L253: ## Rescue Medication Use
L254: The CMINDC variable in the ADCM dataset also indicated treatment for an MS relapse ("MS Relapse Treatment"), and the SCORTFL variable indicated systemic corticosteroids. Per these flags, 39 subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (14.4%) and 72 on teriflunomide (26.3%) received systemic corticosteroids for relapse treatment.
L255: The Applicant's CSR also indicates that methylprednisolone was utilized as a follow-up medication in 4.4% of ublituximab-xiiy subjects and 7.3% of teriflunomide subjects. However, the indication and dose is not accounted for in these summary statistics.
L256: *Reviewer comment: The lower frequency of steroid use in the ublituximab-xiiy group is expected, as this group experienced fewer relapses.*
L257: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L258: The primary efficacy endpoint was Annualized Relapse Rate (ARR), which the applicant calculated as a group ARR utilizing treatment duration in years (STDURY) and number of IRAP-confirmed clinical relapses. The ARR for the ublituximab-xiiy group was 0.09, and for the teriflunomide group was 0.23, for an ARR ratio of 0.41 (95% CI 0.28 to 0.59, p = 2.098e-07) (Table 16). These results indicate a statistically significant treatment effect of ublituximab-xiiy in relapse reduction compared to teriflunomide.

L262: *Reviewer Comment: Reviewer calculations for unadjusted ARR are consistent with those presented by the applicant. The applicant also presented an analysis with LS mean for ARR calculated using negative binomial regression of 0.076 (95% CI 0.042, 0.138) for the ublituximab-xiiy group and 0.188 (95% CI 0.124, 0.283) for the teriflunomide group. The rate ratio was 0.406 (95% CI 0.268, 0.615, p &lt;0.001). Please refer to the statistical review for replication of these adjusted results.*
L263: As demonstrated in Table 16, the double-blind treatment exposure was similar between treatment groups at both the individual and group levels. The percentage of patients who experienced a confirmed relapse during double-blind treatment was higher in the teriflunomide group (24.8%) compared to ublituximab-xiiy (13.3%). The mean (SD) number of confirmed
L264: relapses experienced per patient was higher in the teriflunomide group (0.41 (0.8)) compared to ublituximab-xiiy (0.16 (0.5)).
L265: The applicant also conducted 3 sensitivity analyses for ARR, specifically calculations of ARRs using IRAP-confirmed relapses including follow-up, all relapses irrespective of confirmation, and multiple imputation of withdrawn subjects. These analyses yielded ARR ratios ranging from 0.39 to 0.43 and remained statistically significant, indicating robustness of the primary endpoint in these circumstances.
L266: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L267: There were no issues regarding the quality of the efficacy data submitted. Please refer to the Protocol Deviations discussion earlier in this section regarding protocol violations that raised concerns regarding data integrity. Please also refer to the OSI review by Cara Alfaro, PharmD.
L268: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L269: ## Gadolinium-enhancing lesions
L270: The first key secondary efficacy endpoint was the total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions on brain MRI by Week 96. The mITT-MRI population included subjects who had baseline and post-baseline MRI efficacy assessments (ublituximab-xiiy n = 265, teriflunomide n = 270). Please refer to Table 12 for a summary of baseline gadolinium-enhancing lesions per treatment group.
L271: Per the applicant's analysis, both treatment groups had a mean number of 3.8 MRIs. Ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects had a lower number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions than those on teriflunomide on all post-baseline MRIs. At Week 96, the mean (SD) total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions per MRI scan was 0.03 (0.1) for the ublituximab-xiiy group and 0.82 (1.8) for the teriflunomide group (Table 17). The applicant's analysis yielded a rate ratio for the total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions per MRI scan of 0.033 (95% CI 0.019 to 0.058, p &lt; 0.0001), indicating a statistically significant reduction of gadolinium-enhancing lesions with ublituximab-xiiy compared to teriflunomide.

L276: ## New or enlarging T2 lesions
L277: The second key secondary efficacy endpoint was the total number of new or enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per brain MRI by Week 96.
L278: Per the applicant's analysis, both treatment groups had a mean number of 2.8 MRIs. Ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects had a lower mean number of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions than those on teriflunomide on all post-baseline MRIs. At Week 96, the total mean (SD) total number of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per MRI scan was 0.35 (0.7) for the ublituximab-xiiy group and 4.28 (7.7) for the teriflunomide group (Table 18). The applicant's analysis yielded a rate ratio for the total number of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per MRI scan of 0.076 (95% CI 0.056, 0.104; p &lt; 0.0001), indicating a statistically significant reduction of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions with ublituximab-xiiy compared to teriflunomide.
L279: *Reviewer comment: Based upon the ADMRI dataset, it appears that the Week 12 MRI was not included in the analysis for new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions. However, the difference in the number of MRIs used for each endpoint is not explained in the protocol or Clinical Study Report. An Information Request was sent on 3/28/2022, with a response received on 5/5/2022. The applicant indicated that per protocol, the Week 12 MRI would only be used for gadolinium-enhancing lesion-related endpoints.*

L285: ## Confirmed Disability Progression (12-week)
L286: Please refer to efficacy results presented in Section 6.3.2 for the pre-specified pooled analysis of Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 for 12-week confirmed disability progression.
L287: ## Other Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
L288: Three other secondary efficacy endpoints that are not accepted clinical endpoints, but are considered supportive of the primary efficacy finding, were analyzed by the applicant (i.e., were not replicated by this reviewer).
L289: The applicant compared the proportion of subjects with No Evidence of Disease Activity (NEDA) from Week 24 to Week 96. NEDA was defined as having no IRAP-confirmed relapses, MRI activity (either gadolinium-enhancing or new/enlarging T2 lesions), or 12-week CDP between Weeks 24 and 96. Subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to achieve NEDA (44.6%, n = 121) compared to those receiving teriflunomide (15.0%, n = 41), with a statistically significant odds ratio of 5.44 (95% CI 3.54 to 8.38, p&lt;0.0001) in favor of ublituximab-xiiy.
L290: The applicant also compared the proportion of subjects reaching impairment on the Symbol Digit Modalities Test (SDMT) from baseline to Week 96, defined as a decrease from baseline of at least 4 points at any post-baseline assessment. The applicant's analysis did not indicate a
L291: statistically significant difference in the proportion of subjects reaching impaired SDMT between the ublituximab-xiiy and teriflunomide groups; the odds ratio was 0.87 (95% CI 0.60 to 1.26, p = 0.47), and the proportion of subjects reaching impaired SDMT in the ublituximab-xiiy group was 29.2%, compared to 31.8% in the teriflunomide group.
L292: Finally, the applicant compared percentage change in brain volume from baseline to Week 96 between the treatment groups in the mITT-MRI population, defined as subjects who had baseline and post-baseline MRI efficacy assessments. As demonstrated in Figure 2, brain volume increased slightly at Week 24 in the teriflunomide group, then declined at a rate similar to that of the ublituximab-xiiy group through Week 96. The applicant performed a mixed model repeated measures analysis, which indicated that the percentage change of the cube root transformed volume from baseline at Week 96 was significantly higher in the teriflunomide group compared to ublituximab-xiiy (i.e., in favor of teriflunomide; LS means [ublituximab-xiiy – teriflunomide] -0.072, 95% CI -0.107 to -0.036, p &lt; 0.0001).
L293: Figure 2 (Applicant). Study TG1101-RMS301, Mean Percentage Change in Brain Volume
L294: Source: Figure 14.2.9
L295: Abbreviations: See Section 4.
L296: Note: Mean and the 95% Wald Confidence Intervals are displayed.

L298: ## Dose/Dose Response
L299: Not applicable, as a single dose was studied in Study TG1101-RMS301.
L300: ## Durability of Response
L301: Though the trial was not designed to evaluate durability of response, there did not appear to be a higher frequency of relapse later in the double blind treatment period, suggesting a durable response to ublituximab-xiiy in the setting of B-cell depletion.
L302: ## Persistence of Effect
L303: Again, the trial was not designed to evaluate persistence of effect, but most patients in the ublituximab-xiiy group appeared to experience B-cell depletion through the end of the double blind treatment period, during which multiple doses of ublituximab-xiiy were administered (See Section 8.4.6). Therefore, the data suggests that there is not a meaningful loss of effect of ublituximab-xiiy over time.
L304: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L305: ## Confirmed and Unconfirmed Relapses
L306: Overall, 218 confirmed and unconfirmed relapses occurred across both treatment groups in this study, 152 (69.8%) in the teriflunomide group and 66 (30.3%) in the ublituximab-xiiy group. The rate of IRAP confirmation was similar in both treatment groups, 66.7% (44/66 relapses) in the ublituximab-xiiy group and 73.0% (111/152) in the teriflunomide group. Relapse severity was not captured in the eCRF, and therefore was not able to be tabulated for this review.
L307: The unadjusted group ARR for both confirmed and unconfirmed relapses was 0.14 in the ublituximab-xiiy group versus 0.32 in the teriflunomide group, with an ARR ratio of 0.45 (95% CI 0.33 to 0.60). The percentage of subjects who experienced either a confirmed or unconfirmed relapse was lower in the ublituximab-xiiy group (17.3%) compared to teriflunomide (31.4%). Additionally, the mean (SD) number of relapses (either confirmed or unconfirmed) was lower in the ublituximab-xiiy group (0.24 (0.6)) compared to teriflunomide (0.55 (1.0)).
L308: These relapse data are summarized in Table 19.

L317: ## 6.2. Study TG1101-RMS302
L318: ## 6.2.1. Study Design
L319: ## Overview and Objective
L320: Study TG1101-RMS302 ("ULTIMATE II") was one of two identical randomized controlled trials (RCTs) whose objective was to assess the efficacy and safety of ublituximab-xiiy compared to bioequivalent teriflunomide as treatment for relapsing forms of MS. Please refer to Section 6.1 for Study TG1101-RMS302 trial design information.
L321: ## Trial Design
L322: Study TG1101-RMS302 was identical in design to that of Study TG1101-RMS301. Refer to Section 6.1.
L323: ## Study Endpoints
L324: Study TG1101-RMS302 was identical in design to that of Study TG1101-RMS301. Refer to Section 6.1.
L325: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L326: Study TG1101-RMS302 was identical in design to that of Study TG1101-RMS301. Refer to Section 6.1.
L327: ## Protocol Amendments
L328: The protocols for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 were identical, and protocol amendments were made simultaneously. Please refer to Section 6.1.2 and Table 5. The number of patients randomized under each version of the protocol for TG1101-RMS302 is presented in Table 21.

L332: ## 6.2.2. Study Results
L333: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L334: The Clinical Study Report submitted by the applicant states that the study was conducted in accordance with the protocol and "consensus ethical principles originating or derived from the Declaration of Helsinki and in compliance with all ICH GCP guidelines."
L335: ## Financial Disclosure
L336: The applicant provided Form 3454 indicating that there were no financial arrangements with investigators whereby the value of compensation could be affected by the outcome of the study as defined in 21 CFR 54.2(a). No investigators had significant disclosed interest or were missing disclosure information for Study TG1101-RMS302. See Section 13.3.
L337: ## Patient Disposition
L338: In Study TG1101-RMS302, 629 subjects were screened, 84 were screen failures, and 545 were enrolled and randomized. The ITT population included 545 subjects (272 ublituximab-xiiy, 273 teriflunomide), the mITT population included 544 subjects (272 ublituximab-xiiy, 272 teriflunomide). All randomized subjects were exposed to study treatment.
L339: Overall, 493 (90.5%) subjects completed the 96-week double blind treatment period (254 [93.4%] ublituximab-xiiy, 239 [87.5%] teriflunomide), and 386 (70.8%) subjects completed the follow-up period (199 [73.2%] ublituximab-xiiy, 187 [68.5%] teriflunomide). Premature study drug discontinuation occurred in 52 (9.5%) subjects overall (18 [6.6%] ublituximab-xiiy and 34 [12.5%] teriflunomide). Reasons for premature study drug discontinuation in the ITT population are delineated by treatment group in Table 22.

L397: According to the applicant's CSR, the most commonly utilized follow-up medications were cholestyramine (90.1% ublituximab-xiiy, 86.8% teriflunomide) and gadobutrol (54.8% ublituximab-xiiy, 51.8% teriflunomide). L398: ## Rescue Medication Use
L399: The CMINDC variable in the ADCM dataset also indicated treatment for an MS relapse ("MS Relapse Treatment"), and the SCORTFL indicated systemic corticosteroids. Per these flags, 32 subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (11.8%) and 71 on teriflunomide (26.1%) received systemic corticosteroids for relapse treatment.
L400: The applicant's CSR also indicates that methylprednisolone was utilized as a follow-up medication in 2.6% of ublituximab-xiiy subjects and 5.1% of teriflunomide subjects. However, the indication and dose is not accounted for in these summary statistics.
L401: *Reviewer comment: The lower frequency of steroid use in the ublituximab-xiiy group is expected, as this group experienced fewer relapses.*
L402: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L403: The primary efficacy endpoint was Annualized Relapse Rate (ARR), which the applicant calculated as a group ARR utilizing treatment duration in years (STDURY) and number of IRAP-confirmed clinical relapses. The unadjusted group ARR for the ublituximab-xiiy group was 0.11, and for the teriflunomide group was 0.22, for an ARR ratio of 0.50 (95% CI 0.35, 0.70, p = 3.355e-05) (Table 32). These results indicate a statistically significant treatment effect of ublituximab-xiiy in relapse reduction compared to teriflunomide.

L407: *Reviewer Comment: Reviewer calculations for unadjusted ARR are consistent with those presented by the applicant. The applicant also presented an analysis with LS mean for ARR calculated using negative binomial regression of 0.091 (95% CI 0.049, 0.169) for the ublituximab-xiiy group and 0.178 (95% CI 0.109, 0.291) for the teriflunomide group. The ARR rate ratio was 0.509 (95% CI 0.330, 0.784, p=0.0022). Please refer to the statistical review for replication of these adjusted results.*
L408: As demonstrated in Table 32, the double-blind treatment exposure was similar between treatment groups at both the individual and group levels. The percentage of patients who experienced a confirmed relapse during double-blind treatment was higher in the teriflunomide group (26.5%) compared to ublituximab-xiiy (12.5%). The mean (SD) number of confirmed
L409: relapses experienced per patient was higher in the teriflunomide group (0.38 (0.7)) compared to ublituximab-xiiy (0.19 (0.6)).
L410: The applicant also conducted 3 sensitivity analyses for ARR, specifically calculations of ARR using IRAP-confirmed relapses including follow-up, all relapses irrespective of confirmation, and multiple imputation of withdrawn subjects. These analyses yielded ARR ratios ranging from 0.49 to 0.63 and remained statistically significant, indicating robustness of the primary endpoint in these circumstances.
L411: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L412: There were no issues regarding the quality of the efficacy data submitted. Please refer to the Protocol Deviations discussion earlier in this section regarding protocol violations that raised concerns regarding data integrity. Please also refer to the OSI review by Cara Alfaro, PharmD.
L413: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L414: ## Gadolinium-enhancing lesions
L415: The first key secondary efficacy endpoint was the total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions on brain MRI by Week 96. The mITT-MRI population included subjects who had baseline and post-baseline MRI efficacy assessments (ublituximab-xiiy n = 272, teriflunomide n = 267). Please refer to Table 28 for a summary of baseline gadolinium-enhancing lesions per treatment group.
L416: Per the applicant's analysis, ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects had a mean of 3.8 MRIs and teriflunomide treated subjects had a mean of 3.7 MRIs. Similar to the results of Study TG1101-RMS301, ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects had a lower number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions than those on teriflunomide on all post-baseline MRIs. At Week 96, the mean (SD) total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions per MRI scan was 0.04 (0.2) for the ublituximab-xiiy group and 0.88 (2.2) for the teriflunomide group (Table 33). The applicant's analysis yielded a rate ratio for the total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions per MRI scan of 0.035 (95% CI 0.019 to 0.064, p &lt; 0.0001), indicating a statistically significant reduction of gadolinium-enhancing lesions with ublituximab-xiiy compared to teriflunomide.

L421: ## New or enlarging T2 lesions
L422: The second key secondary efficacy endpoint was the total number of new or enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per brain MRI by Week 96.
L423: Per the applicant's analysis, ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects had a mean of 2.9 MRIs and teriflunomide treated subjects had a mean of 2.8 MRIs. Ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects had a lower mean number of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions than those on teriflunomide on all post-baseline MRIs. At Week 96, the total mean (SD) total number of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions per MRI scan was 0.50 (1.1) for the ublituximab-xiiy group and 4.66 (7.7) for the teriflunomide group (Table 34). The applicant's analysis yielded a rate ratio for the total number of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense per MRI scan of 0.100 (95% CI 0.073, 0.136; p &lt; 0.0001), indicating a statistically significant reduction of new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions with ublituximab-xiiy compared to teriflunomide.

L428: *Reviewer comment: Overall, the key secondary efficacy endpoints support a clinically significant treatment effect of ublituximab-xiiy over teriflunomide. The results of the MRI lesion analyses are expected, consistent with other highly effective treatments for RMS, and are congruent with the relapse-related findings of the primary efficacy analysis.*
L429: ## Confirmed Disability Progression (12-week)
L430: Please refer to efficacy results presented in Section 6.3.2 for the pre-specified pooled analysis of Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 for 12-week confirmed disability progression.
L431: ## Other Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
L432: Three other secondary efficacy endpoints that are not accepted clinical endpoints, but are considered supportive of the primary efficacy finding, were analyzed by the applicant (i.e., were not replicated by this reviewer).
L433: The applicant compared the proportion of subjects with NEDA from Week 24 to Week 96. NEDA was defined as having no IRAP-confirmed relapses, MRI activity (either gadolinium-enhancing or new/enlarging T2 lesions), or 12-week CDP between Weeks 24 and 96. Subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to achieve NEDA (43.0%, n = 117) compared to those receiving teriflunomide (11.4%, n = 31), with a statistically significant odds ratio of 7.95 (95% CI 4.92 to 12.84, p&lt;0.0001) in favor of ublituximab-xiiy.
L434: The applicant also compared the proportion of subjects reaching impairment on the Symbol Digit Modalities Test (SDMT) from baseline to Week 96, defined as a decrease from baseline of at least 4 points at any post-baseline assessment. Similar to the results of Study TG1101-
L435: RMS302, the applicant's analysis did not indicate a statistically significant difference in the proportion of subjects reaching impaired SDMT between the ublituximab-xiiy and teriflunomide groups; the odds ratio was 0.87 (95% CI 0.60 to 1.25, p = 0.43), and the proportion of subjects reaching impaired SDMT in the ublituximab-xiiy group was 29.0%, compared to 31.6% in the teriflunomide group.
L436: Finally, the applicant compared percentage change in brain volume from baseline to Week 96 between the treatment groups in the mITT-MRI population, defined as subjects who had baseline and post-baseline MRI efficacy assessments. As demonstrated in Figure 3, and similar to the results of Study TG1101-RMS301, brain volume increased slightly at Week 24 in the teriflunomide group, then declined at a rate similar to that of the ublituximab-xiiy group through Week 96. The applicant performed a mixed model repeated measures analysis, which indicated that the percentage change of the cube root transformed volume from baseline at Week 96 was not statistically significant different in the teriflunomide group compared to ublituximab-xiiy (LS means [ublituximab-xiiy – teriflunomide] -0.018, 95% CI -0.053 to 0.017, p=0.3108).

L443: ## Dose/Dose Response
L444: Not applicable, as a single dose was studied in Study TG1101-RMS302.
L445: ## Durability of Response
L446: Though the trial was not designed to evaluate durability of response, there did not appear to be a higher frequency of relapse later in the double blind treatment period, suggesting a durable response to ublituximab-xiiy in the setting of B-cell depletion.
L447: ## Persistence of Effect
L448: Again, the trial was not designed to evaluate persistence of effect, but most patients in the ublituximab-xiiy group appeared to experience B-cell depletion through the end of the double blind treatment period, during which multiple doses of ublituximab-xiiy were administered (See Section 8.4.6). Therefore, the data suggests that there is not a meaningful loss of effect of ublituximab-xiiy over time.
L449: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L450: ## Confirmed and Unconfirmed Relapses
L451: Overall, 190 confirmed and unconfirmed relapses occurred across both treatment groups in the double-blind treatment period, 115 (60.5%) in the teriflunomide group and 75 (39.5%) in the ublituximab-xiiy group. The rate of IRAP confirmation was lower in the ublituximab-xiiy group (70.7% (53/75 relapses)) compared to the teriflunomide group (88.7% (102/115 relapses)). Again, relapse severity was not captured in the eCRF, and therefore was not able to be tabulated for this review.
L452: *Reviewer comment: An Information Request regarding the higher rate of relapse confirmation in the teriflunomide group was sent to the applicant on 3/28/2022. The applicant responded 5/5/2022, indicating that a review of patient profiles yielded no evidence that data provided to the IRAP could be unblinding for teriflunomide, except for one case of alopecia. This response was considered reasonable.*
L453: The unadjusted group ARR for both confirmed and unconfirmed relapses was 0.15 in the ublituximab-xiiy group versus 0.25 in the teriflunomide group, with an ARR ratio of 0.63 (95% CI 0.46 to 0.84; p = 0.002). The percentage of subjects who experienced either a confirmed or unconfirmed relapse was lower in the ublituximab-xiiy group (17.3%) compared to teriflunomide (28.3%). Additionally, the mean (SD) number of relapses (either confirmed or unconfirmed) was lower in the ublituximab-xiiy group (0.28 (0.7)) compared to teriflunomide (0.42 (0.8)).
L454: These relapse data are summarized in Table 35.

L487: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L488: These pooled studies were utilized for analysis of time to 12-week CDP only. Analysis and replication of 12-week CDP endpoint is deferred to the Biostatistics reviewer.
L489: Per the applicant's analysis, 12-week CDP occurred in 5.2% of subjects in the ublituximab-xiiy group (n = 28) and 5.9% in the teriflunomide group (n = 32). For time to 12-week CDP, the applicant's analysis indicated a non-statistically significant hazard ratio of 0.843 (95% CI 0.504 to 1.407; p = 0.5099).
L490: The applicant also analyzed time to 24-week CDP, which also yielded non-significant results (hazard ratio 0.657, 95% CI 0.358 to 1.205, p = 0.1716).
L491: *Reviewer comment: The applicant analyzed time to 12- and 24-week confirmed disability improvement (CDI) as an exploratory endpoint, and reported statistically significant hazard ratios in favor of ublituximab-xiiy for both endpoints. However, given the non-significant findings in 12- and 24-week CDP, a significant finding in CDI is not relevant. Additionally, this lack of significance on CDP endpoints may be related to the fact that teriflunomide is known to have an effect on CDP, so it would likely be difficult to achieve superiority to teriflunomide on CDP in the context of this trial design. Ublituximab-xiiy could be non-inferior to teriflunomide in terms of CDP, but it is not possible to determine this given the study design.*

L501: ## 6.4. Study TG1101-RMS201
L502: ## 6.4.1. Study Design
L503: Overview and Objective
L504: Study TG1101-RMS201 was a Phase 2a, 52-week, placebo-controlled, dose-finding, multicenter, sequential cohort study evaluating ublituximab-xiiy in subjects with relapsing MS. Data from this study was submitted to the BLA to support the data from Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.
L505: The primary objectives of the study were to:
L506: - Determine the level of B-cell depletion by ublituximab-xiiy in subjects with RMS
- Determine the optimal dose and infusion time for ublituximab-xiiy in subjects with RMS
L507: The secondary objectives were to:
L508: - Examine the effect of ublituximab-xiiy on the development of new gadolinium-enhancing lesions and new or enlarging T2 lesions at 24 and 48 weeks.
- Evaluate the percent of relapses in ublituximab-xiiy-treated RMS subjects
- Determine immunogenicity
- Evaluate the safety of ublituximab-xiiy, as determined by adverse events and serious adverse events, including MS worsening
L509: Trial Design
L510: Study TG1101-RMS201 was a Phase 2a, 52-week, placebo-controlled, dose-finding, multicenter, sequential cohort study evaluating the pharmacodynamics, efficacy, and safety of ublituximab-xiiy in subjects with relapsing MS.
L511: The study schematic for Study TG1101-RMS201 is presented in Figure 4. The study consisted of a screening period of up to 4 week and a 48-week double-blind treatment period. Subjects who remained stable at the end of the double-blind treatment period were able to enter the open-label extension study, Study TG1101-RMS201E.
L512: Figure 4 (Applicant). Study TG1101-RMS201 Study Schematic
L513: Source: Applicant's Clinical Study Report, Study TG1101-RMS201
L514: The study included 6 treatment cohorts with 8 subjects each (6 randomized to ublituximab-xiiy, and 2 to placebo), which are further described in the *Study Treatment* section below.
L515: ## Key Eligibility Criteria
L516: ## *Key Inclusion Criteria*
L517: - Age 18 to 55 years
- Diagnosis of RMS per 2010 McDonald Criteria
- \ge 2 relapses in prior 2 years or 1 relapse in year prior to screening and/or \ge 1 gadolinium-enhancing lesion
- Active disease
- EDSS 0 to 5.5 (inclusive)
- B-cell counts \ge 5\% of total lymphocytes
- Neurological stability \ge 30 days prior to screening and baseline
- Contraception requirements
L518: ## *Key Exclusion Criteria*
L519: - Treatment with anti-CD20 or B-cell directed treatment within last 12 months
- Any previous treatment with alemtuzumab
- Treatment with teriflunomide within 12 months (unless accelerated elimination procedure completed \ge 90 days prior to screening)
L520: - Treatment with natalizumab or fingolimod within 90 days of screening
- Treatment with glatiramer acetate, interferons, dimethyl fumarate, or glucocorticoids within 30 days of screening
- Pregnant or nursing
- \ge 10 years disease duration if EDSS \le 2.0
- Current evidence or history of infection including PML, tuberculosis, hepatitis B, hepatitis C, or HIV
- Absolute neutrophil count or platelet count outside normal range
- Absolute lymphocyte count &lt;1000/\mu\text{L}
- Lack of immunity to varicella as determined by screening
- Vaccination with live virus within 2 months of randomization
L521: ## Study Treatment
L522: The study included 6 treatment cohorts with 8 subjects each (6 randomized to ublituximab-xiiy, and 2 to placebo). Subjects randomized to ublituximab-xiiy received ublituximab-xiiy on Days 1, 15, and Week 24. Subjects randomized to placebo received placebo on Days 1 and 15, then switched to ublituximab-xiiy at Week 24. Each treatment cohort differed in dose and infusion time, and these parameters are described in Table 38.

L530: dosing regimen and infusion time identical to that of patients who initially received ublituximab-xiiy in their treatment cohort.
L531: The protocol provided guidelines for infusion rate reduction or delay in the setting of severe infusion-related reactions. The protocol required that subjects have normal absolute neutrophil and platelet counts prior to ublituximab-xiiy infusion, as well as AST/ALT &lt;5x ULN. Ublituximab-xiiy was held for up to 48 hours to allow normalization of lab values, and if normalization did not occur, the subject was withdrawn from the study.

L537: ## Relapse Assessment
L538: The protocol defined relapses as “the occurrence of new or worsening neurological symptoms attributable to MS, immediately preceded by a stable or improving neurological state of at least 30 days,” that persist for more than 24 hours in the absence of fever, and are accompanied by objective neurological worsening consistent with an increase \ge 0.5 on the EDSS (if baseline EDSS &gt; 1.0) or an increase \ge 1.0 on the EDSS (if baseline EDSS=0).
L539: Relapse assessment was conducted by the treating investigator at each study visit. Subjects who experienced MS relapse was treated with intravenous methylprednisolone 1g daily for up to 5 days, and underwent an MRI 10 days after steroid treatment completion. Additionally, subjects were reconsented following a protocol-defined relapse.
L540: ## Brain MRI
L541: Brain MRIs with and without gadolinium contrast administration were obtained at screening, Week 24, and Week 48. Subjects who received placebo also underwent brain MRI at Week 4 (prior to the start of ublituximab-xiiy treatment). MRIs were evaluated locally for safety and non-MS pathology, and a report was provided to the treating physician.
L542: *Reviewer comment: The method for quantification of gadolinium-enhancing or new/enlarging T2 lesions is not described in the study protocol. The protocol does not mention central reading of MRIs for efficacy endpoints, but a “Central radiology group” at Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai is included in the list of study personnel (Section 6 of CSR).*
L543: ## Study Endpoints
L544: The primary endpoint was responder rate at Week 4, where responders were defined as subjects who experience \ge 95\% B-cell depletion.
L545: *Reviewer comment: The applicant clarified via Information Request that the values utilized to determine the responder rate were percentage of CD19+ B-cells, rather than an absolute CD19+ B-cell count. The use of a percentage in this context is not appropriate, and it is therefore not possible to interpret the primary endpoint of this study. Additionally, the applicant indicated that absolute CD19+ B-cell count was not collected as part of this study, other than at the screening visit, so it is not possible to perform an appropriate analysis using absolute CD19+ B-cell count.*
L546: Secondary efficacy endpoints were:
L547: - Number of new Gd-enhancing lesions at 24 and 48 weeks
L548: - Number of new or enlarging T2 lesions at 24 and 48 weeks
- Annualized relapse rate (ARR)
- Relapse rate reduction (RRR)
- Percent of relapse free subjects
- Reduction in B cells (CD19+), memory (CD19+CD27+) and naïve (CD19+CD27-) B cells at baseline, day 1 (pre dose) and 2; week 2; Day 15 (pre-dose); week 4 and every 4 weeks thereafter until the next infusion at week 24 (pre-dose and 2 days post-dose) 25, 28, 36, 40, 44 and 48
- To examine sustained B-cell reduction during the first and third infusions
- Additional immune profiling (CD4+, CD8+, IL10 and NK cells) at baseline, day 1 (pre dose) and 2; week 2, Day 15 (pre-dose); week 4 and every 4 weeks thereafter until the next infusion at week 24 (pre-dose and 2 days post dose) 25, 28, 36, 40, 44 and 48
- PK (ADME) profile of ublituximab-xiiy at day 1 (pre-dose); week 2; day 15 (pre-dose); weeks 4, 24 (pre-dose), and 25
L549: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L550: ## Analysis Populations
L551: The Intention-to-Treat population was defined as all registered subjects who received at least one dose of ublituximab-xiiy. The primary efficacy analysis was conducted in the ITT population.
L552: The modified Intention-to-Treat (mITT) population was defined as all subjects who received ublituximab-xiiy and had one baseline and post-baseline MRI.
L553: The safety population was defined as all subjects who received at least one dose of ublituximab-xiiy.
L554: ## Primary Endpoint
L555: The primary efficacy analysis (proportion of subjects who experienced ≥95% B-cell depletion at Week 4) was conducted in the ITT population. The proportion of responders was presented, along with 95% confidence intervals.
L556: ## Key Secondary Endpoints
L557: Each secondary endpoint was summarized for all subjects using descriptive statistics. No comparisons were made between subjects randomized to ublituximab-xiiy or initial placebo.

L564: Source: Applicant's response to Information Request, received 1/24/22
L565: ## 6.4.2. Study Results
L566: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L567: The Clinical Study Report submitted by the applicant states that the study was conducted in compliance with Good Clinical Practice guidelines, outlined in the International Council for Harmonisation E6 Tripartite Guideline and 21 CFR 312.
L568: However, one site (Site CUA, which enrolled 10 subjects) was identified as having “significant Good Clinical Practice violations and other non-compliance issues” by the applicant. Subjects were transferred to Site WLA. Please refer to further discussion of these events under the *Protocol Violations/Deviations* section below. The applicant therefore conducted sensitivity analyses to assess the impact on study data.
L569: ## Financial Disclosure
L570: The applicant provided Form 3454 indicating that there were no financial arrangements with investigators whereby the value of compensation could be affected by the outcome of the study as defined in 21 CFR 54.2(a). No investigators had significant disclosed interest or were missing disclosure information for Study TG1101-RMS201. The applicant was not able to obtain disclosure information for one sub-investigator, and a form of Due Diligence was submitted with Form 3454. The explanation indicated that the sub-investigator did not complete a financial disclosure form prior to leaving the institution, and was added to an updated Form 1572 after trial initiation. See Section 13.3.
L571: ## Patient Disposition
L572: Fifty-six subjects signed informed consent, and 49 were randomized. Thirty-six subjects were randomized to ublituximab-xiiy, and 13 to placebo. Of the 13 subjects randomized to placebo, 12 were subsequently treated with ublituximab-xiiy; one subject was treated with placebo only and discontinued study participation due to an adverse event (low absolute lymphocyte count). Therefore, the placebo safety population included 13 subjects, and the ITT and safety (ublituximab-xiiy) populations included 48 subjects.
L573: Forty-five subjects completed the treatment period (48 weeks) as planned. Three subjects (6.3%) prematurely discontinued study participation due to withdrawal by subject (n = 1), pregnancy (n = 1), and “missing” (n = 1).
L574: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L575: According to the applicant, 32 subjects (66.7%) experienced a major protocol deviation. The reasons for major protocol deviations included missing laboratory assessments (41.7%),
L576: inclusion/exclusion criteria not met (25.0%), informed consent (18.8%; delayed reconsenting), study procedures (12.5%), and investigational product compliance (8.3%).
L577: As mentioned previously, one site (Site CUA, which enrolled 10 subjects) was identified as having “significant Good Clinical Practice violations and other non-compliance issues” by the applicant. An audit certificate was provided with this submission, indicating that an Investigator Site Audit was performed between 9/26/2017 and 9/28/2017. All 10 subjects enrolled at this site experienced major protocol violations, including screening B-cell count not being performed (100%), missing screening labs (30%), informed consent violations (40%), inclusion/exclusion criteria violations (40%), other labs missing (30%), and violations of trial procedures (30%, for example, study visits not done). Two subjects with a history of tuberculosis were enrolled in the trial, one subject with hepatitis C antibody positivity was enrolled, and one subject with prior rituximab use was enrolled.
L578: *Reviewer comment: The major protocol violations reported at Site CUA are concerning and have significant potential to affect the validity of trial results since 20.8% of the ITT population was enrolled at that site. The applicant conducted a sensitivity analysis excluding subjects from this site, the results of which are discussed below.*
L579: ## Table of Demographic Characteristics
L580: Demographic characteristics for the ITT population (all subjects treated with ublituximab-xiiy) are presented in Table 41. Most subjects were female, Caucasian, non-Hispanic, and ≥40 years of age. All subjects were from the United States.

L596: Per the applicant's CSR, 62.5% of subjects in the ITT population had received any MS therapy in the past 5 years. The most commonly used MS therapies in the 5 years prior to trial enrollment were dimethyl fumarate (25.0%), fingolimod (25.0%), glatiramer acetate (18.8%), natalizumab (18.8%), interferon beta-1a (14.6%), and interferon beta-1b (6.3%).
L597: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L598: ## Treatment Compliance
L599: Per the applicant's CSR, in the safety population, 93.8% of subjects received all 3 planned infusions of ublituximab-xiiy; 6.3% of subjects completed 2 infusions.
L600: ## Concomitant Medications
L601: Per the applicant's CSR, the most commonly utilized concomitant medications in the ITT population were diphenhydramine hydrochloride (58.3%), vitamin D (45.8%), methylprednisolone (41.7%), diphenhydramine (39.6%), ibuprofen (39.6%), paracetamol
L602: (39.6%), and dexamethasone (29.2%).
L603: Per the applicant's CSR, the most commonly utilized follow-up medications in the ITT population were glucocorticoids (31.3%, specifically methylprednisolone [18.8%], methylprednisolone sodium succinate [6.3%], and prednisone [4.2%]), vitamin D (58.3%), ibuprofen (33.3%), multivitamins (27.1%), diphenhydramine (25.0%), vitamin B12 (22.9%), gabapentin (22.9%), paracetamol (20.8%), and baclofen (20.8%).
L604: ## Rescue Medications
L605: The ADCM dataset contained a CSRELFL variable, which served as a flag for corticosteroid therapy for MS relapse. However, all rows with CSRELFL=Y were either prophylactic corticosteroids used for infusion pre-medication (100mg IV methylprednisolone), asthma treatment, or prior corticosteroid relapse treatment.
L606: *Reviewer comment: It does not appear that any subjects enrolled in this study received corticosteroids for relapse. However, the CSR indicates that 4 confirmed relapses occurred in this study. The reason for this discrepancy is not clear.*
L607: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L608: The primary endpoint was the B-cell responder rate (defined as B-cell depletion \ge 95\%) in the ITT population at Week 4. B-cell depletion was based upon the CD19+ B-cell ratio, which is not considered interpretable. Absolute CD19+ B-cell count should have been used to determine B-cell depletion and calculate a responder rate.
L609: *Reviewer comment: The applicant clarified via Information Request that the values utilized to determine the responder rate were percentage of CD19+ B-cells, rather than an absolute CD19+ B-cell count. The use of a percentage in this context is not appropriate, and it is therefore not possible to interpret the primary endpoint of this study. Additionally, the applicant indicated that absolute CD19+ B-cell count was not collected as part of this study, other than at the screening visit, so it is not possible to perform an appropriate analysis using absolute CD19+ B-cell count.*
L610: As mentioned previously, one site (Site CUA, which enrolled 10 subjects) was identified as having "significant Good Clinical Practice violations and other non-compliance issues" by the applicant. The applicant therefore conducted sensitivity analyses to assess the impact to study data, and found that there were no meaningful differences in the results with inclusion of this site.
L611: Clinical Review
Laura Baldassari, MD, MHS
BLA 761238
Ublituximab-xiiy (Briumvi)
L612: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L613: One site (Site CUA, which enrolled 10 subjects) was identified as having “significant Good Clinical Practice violations and other non-compliance issues” by the applicant. Subjects were transferred to Site WLA. The applicant therefore conducted sensitivity analyses to assess the impact to study data.
L614: Otherwise, no issues regarding data quality or integrity were identified.
L615: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L616: Secondary efficacy endpoints were not reviewed in detail for this study. The applicant indicated that 4 confirmed relapses occurred during the study, and that 91.7% of subjects were relapse-free at the end of the study.
L617: ## Dose/Dose Response
L618: Though this study involved administration of multiple doses of ublituximab-xiiy, the issues with the primary endpoint (CD19+ B-cells) preclude evaluation of a dose response. This study was not designed to evaluate a potential dose response for clinical endpoints.
L619: ## Durability of Response
L620: The design of this study was not intended to evaluate durability of response. Therefore, this section is not applicable.
L621: ## Persistence of Effect
L622: The design of this study was not intended to evaluate persistence of effect. Therefore, this section is not applicable.
L623: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L624: Not applicable.
L625: ## 7. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L626: ## 7.1. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L627: ## 7.1.1. Primary Endpoints
L628: Both Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 met their primary efficacy endpoint, annualized relapse rate, demonstrating a significant reduction in ARR in subjects who received ublituximab-xiiy compared to those on teriflunomide (Table 44). The ARR ratio for ublituximab-xiiy compared to teriflunomide was 0.41 for Study TG1101-RMS301 and 0.50 for Study TG1101-RMS302.
L629: Table 44 (Reviewer). Summary of Efficacy Results, Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302

L631: ## 7.1.2. Secondary and Other Endpoints
L632: Both Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 met their key secondary efficacy endpoints, total number of gadolinium-enhancing lesions and new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions, demonstrating a significant reduction in MRI lesions in subjects who received ublituximab-xiiy compared to those on teriflunomide (Table 44). However, a significant effect on 12- and 24-week CDP was not demonstrated across the pooled studies.
L633: Other secondary endpoints discussed in Sections 6.1.2 and 6.2.2 are not accepted clinical endpoints, but are considered supportive of the primary efficacy finding. These endpoints included the proportion of subjects with NEDA from Week 24 to 96, the proportion of subjects reaching impaired SDMT from baseline to Week 96, and percentage change in brain volume from baseline to Week 96. Ublituximab-xiiy was statistically superior to teriflunomide in terms
L634: of the NEDA endpoint, but not SDMT or brain volume endpoints.
L635: ## 7.1.3. Subpopulations
L636: Subgroup analysis for ARR was conducted by the applicant and Xiang Ling, PhD, FDA Statistical Reviewer. According to the FDA Statistical Review, the rate ratio for ARR was generally similar across subgroups based on sex and age category (Table 45). The subpopulation analysis by region did suggest benefit of teriflunomide over ublituximab-xiiy in the US/Western Europe population, which contradicted the main analysis, but this analysis is limited by small sample size. There is no biological plausibility for a differential effect of an anti-CD20 therapy in the US/Western Europe population compared to an Eastern European population. The Statistical Review also indicated that this result was inconclusive and likely limited by small sample size. Meaningful subpopulation analysis by race was precluded by the small number of non-Caucasian participants enrolled in the clinical studies.
