L640: ## 7.1.4. Dose and Dose-Response
L641: Not applicable, as one dose was studied in the pivotal trials.
L642: ## 7.1.5. Onset, Duration, and Durability of Efficacy Effects
L643: Ublituximab-xiiy appears to have a relatively rapid onset of clinical effects, as subjects on ublituximab-xiiy experienced a rapid depletion in CD19+ B-cells, reduction in MRI lesion activity, and few relapses. As discussed in the sections above, these studies were not designed to
L644: evaluate duration and durability of efficacy effects, but it appears that the B-cell depletion associated with ublituximab-xiiy, which is presumed to result in its clinical benefit on relapses, was durable throughout the study.
L645: ## 7.2. Additional Efficacy Considerations
L646: ## 7.2.1. Considerations on Benefit in the Postmarket Setting
L647: There are no specific considerations for the benefit in the postmarket setting.
L648: ## 7.2.2. Other Relevant Benefits
L649: There are no additional relevant benefits.
L650: ## 7.3. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L651: Ublituximab-xiiy demonstrated clear, statistically significant, and robust efficacy findings on ARR, gadolinium-enhancing lesions, and new/enlarging T2 hyperintense lesions compared to teriflunomide.
L652: ## 8. Review of Safety
L653: ## 8.1. Safety Review Approach
L654: The safety review will focus on the double-blind portion of the Phase 3 Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, as well as their open-label extension (TG1101-RMS303). The results of Study TG1101-RMS201 and its open label-extension, TG1101-RMS201E, will be discussed as well. The applicant's Integrated Summary of Safety was separated into Phase 2 and Phase 3 datasets, as previously agreed upon by the Division.
L655: Following Division feedback at the pre-BLA meeting, the definition of a treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE) was revised to "AEs with an onset on, or after the first dose of study medication or with an onset prior to the first dose of study medication that increases in severity on, or after the first dose of study medication
L656: The applicant-provided datasets were initially evaluated by the Office of Computational Science (OCS) JumpStart team, and no major issues were identified. Dr. Rui Li, Senior Clinical Analyst, performed several of the reported safety analyses in conjunction with this reviewer.

L672: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of the safety database
L673: The ublituximab-xiiy RMS trial safety database is adequately sized with a sufficient number of patients treated for an appropriate duration to assess safety. The demographic characteristics of the RMS trial patients were similar to the intended treatment population, although there
L674: were limited patients in the extremes of ages and in race groups other than White.
L675: ## 8.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L676: ## 8.3.1. Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L677: The level of clinically relevant details provided in narratives submitted with this BLA was limited. Review of pertinent events required reference to CRFs and information previously submitted to the IND (specifically the response to Information Request regarding serious infections, submitted to IND 127265 on 12/5/2019, SDN 103). Additionally, Information Requests sent to the applicant on 2/22/2022 and 3/8/2022 requested revised SAE narratives, as the Patient Profiles that the applicant referenced also did not provide an adequate level of detail. Specifically, the applicant was asked to revise narratives that include a "chronological, detailed, and integrated account of clinical symptoms, laboratory results, radiology results, as well as the subject's clinical course." The applicant responded on 3/25/2022 for ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects and on 4/1/2022 for teriflunomide-treated subjects. This information, which was considered a Major Amendment because it contained new information that obligated additional review time, is incorporated into the narratives discussed in Section 8.4.2.
L678: The application of CTCAE severity grading in these trials was also problematic. In Study TG1101-RMS301, 25 of 59 SAEs were rated as CTCAE Grade 1 (n = 6) or 2 (n = 19), which is not appropriate, as SAEs by definition are considered Grade 3 or above. In Study TG1101-RMS302, 33 of 59 SAEs were rated as CTCAE Grade 1 (n = 8) or 2 (n = 25). An Information Request was sent to the applicant on 3/28/2022 to request an explanation for the discrepant CTCAE grading for SAEs designated as &lt; Grade 3. The applicant responded on 5/5/2022, indicating that AE grading was provided by Study Investigators, and that training and guidance regarding such reporting and grading was provided by the applicant. Additionally, the applicant stated that in some countries, hospitalization is standard for access to care, so hospitalization may trigger an AE to be considered serious per the protocol definition, but did not contribute to the severity grading of the AE. In some cases, the applicant was not able to confirm investigator grading based on available information. The applicant indicated that SAEs designated as Grade 1 or 2 was relatively balanced across the treatment arms (50% ublituximab-xiiy and 48% teriflunomide).
L679: The applicant subsequently conducted a sensitivity analysis in response to this IR, in order to determine whether the overall safety conclusions would change if all Grade 1 or 2 SAEs were considered Grade 3. With this updated grading, the percentage of subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy who experienced a ≥Grade 3 TEAE under the Infections and Infestations SOC increased from 3.7% to 5.0% (versus 2.0% to 3.1% for teriflunomide). The overall safety conclusions did not appear to be affected.
L680: *Reviewer comment: We acknowledge the applicant's explanation for the discrepancies,*
L681: *but upon review, we disagree with the assigned severity of these events based on inconsistency with established CTCAE severity definitions. However, it does not appear that the overall safety conclusions would change based upon this issue.*
L682: Additionally, for both deaths and SAEs related to infections, the applicant consistently assessed causality as not related to ublituximab-xiiy, even though infection is a known risk of other anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody therapies and therefore would be presumed to be a risk with ublituximab-xiiy. The data submitted in this BLA do provide adequate evidence of ublituximab-xiiy acting as an immune suppressant. This lack of attribution was especially evident with serious cases of COVID-19, as the applicant attributed the events to "exposure to infected contacts in the novel COVID-19 pandemic" in safety reports submitted to IND 127265 and did not acknowledge a role of ublituximab-xiiy in the severity of infection or interference with vaccine response.
L683: *Reviewer comment: Infections are a known risk with immunosuppressant treatment such as ublituximab-xiiy, and the lack of insight from the applicant regarding this relationship was concerning. This clinical safety review concludes that ublituximab-xiiy is an immune suppressant and that labeling should enumerate the risk of infection with ublituximab-xiiy.*
L684: ## 8.3.2. Categorization of Adverse Events
L685: ## 8.3.3. Routine Clinical Tests
L686: Routine hematologic tests included complete blood counts (CBC) with differential, including white blood cell, absolute neutrophil, and lymphocyte counts. Absolute CD19+ B-cell counts were regularly assessed in Studies TG1101-RMS301 and 302, but not TG1101-RMS201. Clinical chemistry included basic metabolic panels (BMP), liver function testing (AST, ALT, bilirubin, ALP), amylase, and lipase. Total IgG and IgM were collected at regular intervals. Timing of key safety assessments is provided in Table 4.
L687: ## 8.4. Safety Results
L688: ## 8.4.1. Deaths
L689: ## Study TG1101-RMS301
L690: Two deaths occurred in Study TG1101-RMS301, both in the ublituximab-xiiy group (0.7%).
L691: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 32 year-old woman with no medical history receiving ublituximab-xiiy who experienced the SAEs measles and encephalitis ("acute encephalitis of infectious-allergic etiology [post-measles, post-rubella]") and died on [REDACTED](b) (6). She
L692: received ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED] (b) (6)
L693: She was hospitalized [REDACTED] (b) (6) after developing the SAE measles on [REDACTED] (Study Day 365). Her symptoms initially included generalized weakness and rhinorrhea, then on [REDACTED] (b) (6) she developed rash, fever, headache, cough, and conjunctivitis leading to hospitalization due to measles. Chest X-ray was normal. She was also diagnosed with acute bronchitis.
L694: She was vaccinated against measles during childhood per local standards, but no further vaccinations were administered in adulthood and no antibody titers were measured.
L695: Treatment for this SAE [REDACTED] (b) (6) included ceftriaxone, dexamethasone, furosemide, metoclopramide hydrochloride, sorbitol, fluconazole, unspecified herbal and traditional medicine, and tobramycin. The event was considered recovered/resolved on [REDACTED] (b) (6) and the subject was discharged from the hospital.
L696: She then experienced an investigator-confirmed MS relapse on [REDACTED] (b) (6) which was characterized by left sided weakness and ataxia, gait ataxia, and left hand muscle cramps ("hyperkinesia of the left hand") beginning on [REDACTED] (b) (6). She presented for an unscheduled study visit on [REDACTED] (b) (6), at which time the treating neurologist confirmed a relapse.
L697: Brain MRI was notable for "microfocal brain lesion, which was characteristic of demyelinating disease, stable disease type, non-active phase. In the centrum semiovale, multiple demyelinating lesions from 3 mm to 11 mm were detected in the periventricular brain white matter. Some lesions were T1 hypointense lesions, which indicates that they are old. Similar lesions with indistinct boundaries were noted in the infratentorial region at the level of the middle cerebellar peduncles and in the dorsal region of the pons. Following IV contrast, no signs of pathological gadolinium contrast uptake were detected."
L698: She was treated for this MS relapse from [REDACTED] (b) (6) with methylprednisolone. On [REDACTED] (b) (6), she experienced tonic-clonic seizures with loss of consciousness and was hospitalized. She underwent lumbar puncture, and CSF analysis results were "colorless, transparent, protein 0.166, chlorides 138, glucose 3.8, and cytosis 3." Her neurological examination was notable for end-gaze horizontal nystagmus on left gaze, diplopia, left-sided spasticity, and "involuntary compulsive movement of the left limbs and occasional clonic seizures." EEG on [REDACTED] (b) (6) revealed "irritations effects, signs of convulsive readiness of the brain and high threshold of excitability."
L699: Due to her worsening condition and suspicion of encephalitis, she was transferred to another hospital on [REDACTED] (b) (6). She was noted to be conscious, oriented to time and place,
L700: and had "compulsive movements of the left limbs." Repeat lumbar puncture on [REDACTED](b) (6) was notable for pink and cloudy CSF, with a neutrophilic pleocytosis (22, units not provided), "RBCs per whole field of view," protein "0.495g" (presumably g/L, normal), glucose 4.0 mmol/L (normal), negative HSV-1/2, CMV, EBV, and Parvovirus B19. She worsened despite receiving antiviral therapy.
L701: She was transferred to another hospital on [REDACTED](b) (6) due to worsening condition and seizures, and was noted to have tonic-clonic seizures every 10 minutes. Progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy (PML) was suspected, but brain MRI was not performed due to severe seizures. She was also diagnosed with left sided pneumonia, hydrothorax, and mediastinitis on [REDACTED](b) (6) then bilateral pneumonia on [REDACTED](b) (6)
L702: She became comatose on [REDACTED](b) (6), and repeat CSF was collected for JCV testing. She began treatment with IVIg on [REDACTED](b) (6). She remained comatose and required mechanical ventilation. She experienced dermatitis of the face and cheilitis of unclear etiology. CSF JCV PCR was negative on [REDACTED](b) (6). Measles virus PCR was negative (presumably CSF) on [REDACTED](b) (6). Rubella IgM was negative, and IgG was positive on [REDACTED](b) (6)
L703: Laboratory evaluations during this hospitalization [REDACTED](b) (6) were notable for leukocytosis (WBC 18.1 x109/L), lymphopenia (ALC 0.4 x109/L), and IgG 28.58 g/L (2858 mg/dL, likely elevated post-IVIg).
L704: Non-serious concomitant TEAEs during this event included breast disorder (since [REDACTED](b) (6)), dyskinesia, respiratory failure, pneumonia aspiration, pleurisy, dermatitis, cheilitis, amylase increased, sinus tachycardia, pericardial effusion, arteriosclerotic retinopathy, and dermal cyst. Treatment for this SAE [REDACTED](b) (6) included diazepam, mannitol, furosemide, valproic acid, acyclovir, dexamethasone, IVIg, fluconazole, omeprazole, cefepime, trazodone, clemastine fumarate, prednisolone, pantoprazole, thiopental sodium, valaciclovir, nadroparin, paracetamol, imipenem, levetiracetam, methimazole, pRBCs, diclofenac, acetazolamide, and amiodarone. She also experienced the AE bilateral inferior pneumonia (aspiration) on [REDACTED](b) (6), for which she received amikacin, ceftazidime, and linezolid.
L705: Her condition continued to worsen, pneumonia worsened on chest X-ray, and she required artificial ventilation on [REDACTED](b) (6). She died on [REDACTED](b) (6). She was ultimately diagnosed with post-measles encephalitis (Preferred Term: encephalitis, verbatim term: "acute encephalitis of infectious-allergic etiology (post-measles, post-rubella)"). Autopsy findings included acute meningoencephalitis (possibly of measles, rubella etiology), bilateral interstitial giant cell pneumonia, disseminated intravascular coagulation, bilateral fibrinous pleuropneumonia, encephalomalacia, bilateral hydrothorax, and hydropericarditis. The direct cause of death was reported as "total encephalomalacia...having developed due to
L706: brain coma and continuous artificial ventilation therapy."
L707: The investigator assessed the event encephalitis at not related to study drug.
L708: *Reviewer comment: Please note that the narrative provided in the BLA submission lacked many relevant clinical details, and some of the information reported above was provided in a Response to Information Request regarding serious infections under IND 127265, received on 12/5/2019 (SDN 103), then via Response to Information Request under this BLA received on 3/31/2022.*
L709: *The timing of her encephalitis presentation a few weeks following measles raises suspicion for post-measles acute disseminated encephalomyelitis (ADEM), as measles encephalitis typically occurs within a few days of the rash. In contrast, subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE) presents years after the initial infection. Post-measles ADEM occurs in up to 1 in 1000 measles cases, and is associated with up to 25% mortality.24 However, the lack of gadolinium enhancement on brain MRI is slightly atypical for ADEM, but may be confounded by recent steroid administration for a presumed MS relapse. Patients with ADEM generally have lymphocytic pleocytosis and elevated protein, but this patient was noted to have neutrophilic pleocytosis and normal protein.*
L710: *Giant cell interstitial pneumonia is a rare complication of measles that can develop in immunocompromised patients.*
L711: *This event is unusually severe and unexpected for an otherwise healthy young woman with RMS. Post-measles encephalitis/ADEM is a definite possibility, but atypical features raise suspicion for other infectious or autoimmune etiologies. It is likely that ublituximab-xiiy contributed to the patient's immunocompromised status and therefore is likely associated with the initiation and severity of this event.*
L712: 2. Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was a 22 year-old woman with no medical history receiving ublituximab-xiiy who experienced the SAEs ectopic pregnancy and salpingitis ("left-sided purulent salpingitis") and died on [REDACTED](b)(6). She received ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b)(6)
L713: She was found to have ectopic pregnancy in [REDACTED](b)(6), resulting in hospitalization following ultrasound on [REDACTED](b)(6). She underwent left-sided tubotomy and salpingostomatomplasty, and the event was reported recovered/resolved upon hospital discharge on [REDACTED](b)(6). She was also noted to have anemia at the time, with hemoglobin 90 g/L.
L714: Per the MedWatch reports (UA-PSI-2020-(b)(6), UA-PSI-2020-(b)(6) and UA-PSI-2020-(b)(6) submitted to IND 127265 on (b)(6) (SDN 195) in response to an Information Request, it appears that this subject did not return for her post-surgical 1-month follow-up. She developed left lower abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, fever, and constipation in early (b)(6), and her condition deteriorated leading to hospitalization.
L715: On (b)(6), she was hospitalized for salpingitis and infected post-surgical wound, with infiltrate of the abdominal cavity, partial intestinal obstruction, and “endogenic intoxication.” She received treatment with broad spectrum antibiotics, and underwent left-sided tubotomy and abscess drainage, then three repeat laparotomies (b)(6). Treatment included ceftriaxone, metronidazole, ciprofloxacin, fluconazole, furosemide, paracetamol, morphine, cefotaxime, prednisolone, gentamicin, amikacin, and meropenem. She died on (b)(6).
L716: Autopsy on (b)(6) indicated that the cause of death was “sepsis, toxicosis, perforation of the sigmoid colon and small intestine, peritonitis, pelvioperitonitis, abdominal abscesses and left-sided purulent salpingitis.” Left-sided purulent salpingitis was listed as the main cause of death, and the other findings were listed as complications. Bilateral abscess-forming lower lobe pneumonia, septic splenic hyperplasia, and cachexia were also documented.
L717: The investigator assessed the event at not related to study drug.
L718: *Reviewer comment: Again, this event is unusually severe and unexpected for an otherwise healthy young woman with RMS. Though the lack of post-surgical follow-up likely contributed to evolution to severe infection, immunosuppression from ublituximab-xiiy likely also contributed to the development and severity of infection.*
L719: *Please note that the narrative provided in the BLA submission lacked many relevant clinical details, and the information reported above was provided in a Response to Information Request under IND 127265, received on 8/25/2020 (SDN 195), then a Response to Information Request for this BLA received on 3/31/2022.*
L720: ## Study TG1101-RMS302
L721: One death occurred in Study TG1101-RMS302 in a subject receiving ublituximab-xiiy. Subject (b)(6) was a 36 year-old man with a history of chronic sinusitis and maxillary sinus cyst (since 2016) who experienced the SAE pneumonia (verbatim term “bilateral purulent pneumonia with confluent foci”) and died on (b)(6). He received ublituximab-xiiy on (b)(6).
L722: He experienced the SAE pneumonia (Grade 5) on [REDACTED](b)(6) (Study Day 476) after presenting with cough and fever. He experienced shortness of breath, decreased exercise tolerance, and elevated body temperature for 7 to 10 days prior to hospitalization. He was hospitalized on [REDACTED](b)(6), following outpatient treatment with azithromycin, due to dyspnea, cyanosis, and diffuse edema. Vital signs included blood pressure of 90/60 mmHg and heart rate of 115 bpm. Other ongoing TEAEs included prostatitis, pulmonary edema, hydrothorax, gastric ulcer, pancreatitis hemorrhagic, tracheobronchitis, and left ventricular hypertrophy. Laboratory results were notable for leukocytosis (WBC 13.4 x 109/L) and elevated ESR of 22 mm/msec.
L723: Treatment for this SAE included azithromycin, paracetamol, arginine, meldonium, meropenem, enoxaparin, digoxin, epinephrine, dexamethasone, metronidazole, and electrolyte solutions. Despite treatment, his condition worsened.
L724: He died on [REDACTED](b)(6) and the autopsy listed the direct cause of death as pulmonary and cardiac insufficiency as a complication of bilateral purulent pneumonia with confluent foci. Autopsy findings included bilateral purulent pneumonia with confluent foci, purulent tracheobronchitis, edema and swelling of the brain matter, "parenchymatous degeneration and venous congestion of internal organs, pulmonary edema, bilateral hydrothorax, acute gastric ulcers, and fine-focal hemorrhagic edematous pancreatitis."
L725: The investigator assessed this event as possibly related to ublituximab-xiiy.
L726: *Reviewer comment: Again, this event is unusually severe and unexpected for an otherwise healthy young man with RMS. Immunosuppression from ublituximab-xiiy likely also contributed to the development and severity of infection.*
L727: *Please note that the narrative provided in the BLA submission lacked many relevant clinical details (e.g., autopsy findings, symptoms), and the information reported above was provided in a Response to Information Request under IND 127265, received on 8/25/2020 (SDN 195), then a Response to Information Request for this BLA received on 3/31/2022.*

L733: As of 10/29/2021, one subject in Study TG1101-RMS201E (Subject [REDACTED](b) (6)), a 45 year-old man treated with ublituximab-xiiy from [REDACTED](b) (6) died due to COVID-19 pneumonia. He was treated with ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6)
L734: He was vaccinated against COVID-19 on [REDACTED](b) (6). He developed fever, myalgias, and malaise following exposure to an infected contact on [REDACTED](b) (6), then was diagnosed with COVID-19 on [REDACTED](b) (6). He was noted to have a productive cough with intermittent hemoptysis. He was treated with steroids and a monoclonal antibody infusion. He continued to worsen and experienced hypoxia with oxygen saturations between 80 to 90%. He was hospitalized on [REDACTED](b) (6) with worsening dyspnea, hypoxia (oxygen saturation 81%), and bradycardia. Chest CT showed a “possible left upper lobe segmental pulmonary embolus considered possibly chronic and extensive bilateral ground-glass opacities likely representing multifocal pneumonia, which was considered secondary to the COVID-19 infection.”
L735: He was discharged on home oxygen, but then on [REDACTED](b) (6) he was rehospitalized due to fever and hypoxia. He was diagnosed with acute respiratory failure, pulmonary embolus, and cryptogenic organizing pneumonia. Chest X-ray showed worsening infiltrates. He was also found to have bilateral lower extremity deep vein thrombosis. He stabilized and was discharged on [REDACTED](b) (6), but on [REDACTED](b) (6) he returned and chest CT showed worsening infiltrates. He required BiPAP for respiratory support, then a ventilator on [REDACTED](b) (6). He died on [REDACTED](b) (6). An autopsy was not performed, but the cause of death was listed as multi organ dysfunction syndrome, septic shock, acute respiratory distress syndrome, and COVID-19 pneumonia.
L736: The event was not considered related to ublituximab-xiiy per the Investigator.
L737: **Reviewer comment:** *Immunosuppression from ublituximab-xiiy likely also contributed to the development and severity of infection. Please refer to Section 8.5.7 for discussion of COVID-19.*

L750: ## Serious Infections
L751: - Two fatal infection-related SAEs (salpingitis, measles and encephalitis) are discussed in Section 8.4.1.
L752: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (a 32 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE central nervous system enteroviral infection (Grade 4, "life-threatening infectious meningoencephalomyelitis of enteroviral origin") on [REDACTED](b) (6). She began treatment with ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6), and received subsequent infusions on [REDACTED](b) (6).
L753: She reported onset of myalgia, subjective fever, and allodynia on [REDACTED](b) (6) followed by chills, fever, paresthesia, nausea, vomiting, oscillopsia, and diplopia on [REDACTED](b) (6). She was hospitalized on [REDACTED](b) (6). Neurological examination was notable for diplopia, dysarthria, pseudodystonia of the right arm, and propriospinal myoclonus. EEG "confirmed subcortical symptoms." She improved with diazepam and carbamazepine, and also received IVg acyclovir, and ribavirin. MRI demonstrated "basal and parietal meningitis," and CSF enterovirus PCR was positive. She then "had a low level of consciousness with no response to verbal or painful stimuli" on [REDACTED](b) (6), but regained consciousness on [REDACTED](b) (6). She had neurological improvement and was discharged on [REDACTED](b) (6) with intensive rehabilitation. As of [REDACTED](b) (6), she was improving. The event was reported as recovered/resolved with sequelae on [REDACTED](b) (6). Ublituximab-xiiy was permanently discontinued. The investigator considered this event as possibly related to ublituximab-xiiy.
L754: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (a 37 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE meningoencephalitis viral (Grade 3, "viral meningoencephalitis") on [REDACTED](b) (6). She began treatment with ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6), and received subsequent infusions on [REDACTED](b) (6). Approximately 2 months after her most recent infusion, she developed fever, vomiting, headaches and weakness. She was hospitalized for viral meningoencephalitis on [REDACTED](b) (6). MRI demonstrated "[a visible irregular area in the left central cerebellar peduncle] with slightly increased signal intensity...which may be consistent with recent demyelination lesions...[and] very numerous motion artifacts." New MS lesions were noted by the treating physician. No signs of meningeal infiltration were noted, so a diagnosis of encephalitis was considered. CSF was notable for lymphocytic pleocytosis (75 cells/\muL, 97%), elevated protein 90 mg/dL, and "high glucose." CSF Lyme IgG/M were negative. PML was considered in the differential, and JCV IgG testing was "borderline positive" and less than a result from [REDACTED](b) (6). However, CSF was not tested for JCV PCR due to lack of availability at the site. Subsequent lumbar punctures (LPs) were performed on [REDACTED](b) (6), and meningitis panels were negative. Tick-borne encephalitis IgG/M were negative. All infectious studies were negative. Previous TEAEs included lymphocyte count decreased (ALC 0.3 \times 10^9/L on [REDACTED](b) (6)), but her ALC recovered and was 1.1 \times 10^9/L on [REDACTED](b) (6).
L755: She was treated with acyclovir, paracetamol, ceftriaxone, enoxaparin, pantoprazole, dexamethasone, lactulose, probiotics, hydroxyzine, and prednisone. She was hospitalized from [REDACTED](b) (6) and was transferred to inpatient rehabilitation. She
L756: improved and “was no longer bedridden” and was “mobile with the use of walker.” The treating physician considered concurrent viral meningitis and an MS relapse as a diagnosis. Ublituximab-xiiy was permanently discontinued.
L757: *Reviewer comment: This case of viral meningoencephalitis is somewhat unusual, particularly since the subject was apparently severely impaired (“bedridden”) and no infectious etiology was identified. A concurrent MS relapse is possible given the reported MRI findings, but would be unusual in this context. The subject’s JCV antibody titer was intermediate, and JCV PCR in CSF was not tested, so it is not possible to rule out PML based upon the information provided. However, the reported CSF profile (moderate lymphocytic pleocytosis, elevated glucose) is not typical for PML.*
L758: - Subject [REDACTED] (a 32 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE central nervous system enteroviral infection (Grade 3, “meningoencephalitis of enterovirus etiology”) in [REDACTED]. She began treatment with ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED] and received subsequent infusions on [REDACTED]. [REDACTED] months after her most recent infusion, she developed fever and urinary urgency, and was diagnosed with a UTI for which she was treated with antibiotics. Following completion of her antibiotic course, her fever returned and she had dizziness and emesis.
L759: She was hospitalized on [REDACTED], then transferred to another hospital on [REDACTED]. Brain and cervical spine MRI showed “signs of demyelinating process with no MRI signs of activity and signs of posterior cranial fossa meningitis (granulomatous process could not be eliminated).” She was “conscious, but lethargic.” Chest CT showed “multiple small foci in both lungs (including subpleural and peribronchial foci), hyperdense focal node in sternum, which were suggestive of inflammatory changes in both lungs.” Retinal angiopathy was noted on ophthalmological examination. CSF obtained on [REDACTED] showed elevated protein (1.15 g/L, reference 0.15 to 0.45), and potentially a pleocytosis (notation and units not clear in narrative). She was diagnosed with neurosarcoidosis presenting as meningoencephalitis.
L760: Her condition deteriorated, and she became comatose. Brain MRI showed “cortical cerebellar sulci were diffusely flattened and hyperintense signal on T2-WI in the cerebral cortex of the cerebellum. With contrast enhancement, a pronounced vascular pattern in the meninges of the cerebellar hemispheres (hyperemia) and leptomeningeal contrast uptake were evident.” Repeat CSF showed elevated glucose (4.6 mmol/L, reference 0.15 to 0.45) and elevated protein (0.96 g/L, reference 0.15 to 0.45). One blood test was positive for enterovirus on PCR, but no other infectious evaluations were positive.
L761: She was treated with IVIg, vinpocetine, “polypeptides of the cerebral cortex of cattle” (Cortexin), citicoline, ipidacrine, betahistine, choline alfoscerate, ursodeoxycholic acid,
L762: mebicar, fosfomycin, maprotiline, mannitol, methylethylpiridinol, fluconazole, omeprazole, lactobacillus acidophilus, insulin, acetazolamide, betahistine, glucose, metoclopramide, paracetamol, glycine, levofloxacin, linezolid, meropenem, nadroparin, voriconazole, dexamethasone, and ofloxacin.
L763: The event was reported as recovered/resolved on [REDACTED](b) (6), and she was discharged from the hospital. Ublituximab-xiiy was permanently discontinued on [REDACTED](b) (6).
L764: *Reviewer comment: The final diagnosis for this event is not clear based on the provided information. Her presentation, MRI findings, and CSF appear consistent with a meningoencephalitis. However, the basis for a neurosarcoidosis diagnosis is not provided, as the chest CT did not demonstrate hilar adenopathy and no biopsy was performed. Additionally, concurrent MS and neurosarcoidosis would be extremely uncommon. The report of a positive enterovirus PCR in blood is of unclear significance to her presentation, but given the other cases of enteroviral meningitis reported in this development program, this is a possible etiology.*
L765: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (a 32 year-old man receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE pulmonary tuberculosis (Grade 2, "infiltrative pulmonary tuberculosis in the phase of destruction") on [REDACTED](b) (6). He began treatment with ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6), and received subsequent infusions on [REDACTED](b) (6). He underwent Diaskintest, a tuberculosis screening method in Russia, which was negative on [REDACTED](b) (6). He was hospitalized on [REDACTED](b) (6) due to cough. Chest CT showed infiltrative pulmonary tuberculosis. He was diagnosed with tuberculosis on [REDACTED](b) (6) and was treated with isoniazid, pyrazinamide, ethambutol, and rifampicin. The event was recovered/resolved with sequelae on [REDACTED](b) (6) and the subject was discharged from the hospital. He was withdrawn from the study due to this SAE.
L766: *Reviewer comment: The serious infections reported in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy appear qualitatively more severe than those reported in subjects receiving teriflunomide, and also occurred at a higher frequency (5.5% versus 2.2%). The central nervous system viral infections (including one leading to death) are unusually severe and concerning, particularly in otherwise healthy, young RMS patients. Additionally, several SAEs are classified as Grade 2. Since these events were severe, medically significant, and/or required hospitalization, event should be considered at least Grade 3 based upon CTCAE definitions. See Section 8.3.1.*

L771: *Reviewer comment: As presented in Section 8.4.6, subjects on ublituximab-xiiy appeared to have a sharp decrease in lymphocytes on Study Day 2, which is not unexpected given its mechanism of action. This subject appeared to recover spontaneously despite continued treatment, which is consistent with the median ALC trends noted for both Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.*

L774: "presumptive positive" then negative at baseline, then "presumptive positive" and positive at Week 3 Day 15. She was withdrawn from the study on [REDACTED](b)(6), due to "investigator or sponsor decision."

- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (a 46 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE chronic hepatitis B (Grade 3) on [REDACTED](b) (6). L793: She was hospitalized due to nausea, vomiting, dark urine, and jaundice on [REDACTED](b) (6). L794: *Reviewer comment: This case meets Hy's Law criteria, but is due to hepatitis B rather than ublituximab-xiiy directly. This subject should not likely have been included in this study due to hepatitis B surface antigen at screening, though confirmatory testing was negative. Screening laboratories did not include other hepatitis B testing. Hepatitis B reactivation is a known risk with other anti-CD20 treatments, so reactivation was likely the result of ublituximab-xiiy treatment.*
L795: ## Cytopenias
L796: - Subject [REDACTED] (a 35 year-old man receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE febrile neutropenia (Grade 3) on [REDACTED](b) (6). He received his first dose of ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6) and a subsequent infusion on [REDACTED](b) (6). He was hospitalized on [REDACTED](b) (6) due to febrile neutropenia, worsening of hidradenitis suppurativa, and toothache. Blood cultures were negative. However, culture of a swab of an ulcer (unclear location, possibly related to hidradenitis suppurativa) grew "various bacterial colonies with a predominance of *E. Coli*" resistant to several antibiotics. He was treated with piperacillin/tazobactam, clindamycin, and filgrastim from [REDACTED](b) (6) laboratory evaluation was notable for leukopenia (WBCs 1.4 \times 10^9/L) and an ANC of 0.2 \times 10^9/L. The event was recovered/resolved, and he was discharged from the hospital on [REDACTED](b) (6). Study treatments were permanently discontinued. Concurrent TEAEs included toothache, which was considered as a possible source of infection in addition to a scrotal lesion. The study site reported that the subject was experiencing agranulocytosis due to methimazole. Per the Patient Profile, "nolotil (metamizol)" was initiated for toothache on [REDACTED](b) (6) and discontinued on [REDACTED](b) (6).
L797: *Reviewer comment: Metamizol is a pain medication with a known risk of agranulocytosis, and the timing of the TEAE febrile neutropenia is suggestive of a relationship with this drug rather than ublituximab-xiiy, as indicated by the investigator.*
L798: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (a 39 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE
L799: bone marrow toxicity (Grade 4, "possible toxic bone marrow damage") on [REDACTED](b)(6), which was documented as resolved on [REDACTED](b)(6). She received her first dose of ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b)(6), with subsequent infusions on [REDACTED](b)(6), and [REDACTED](b)(6). The narrative indicates that this subject experienced agranulocytosis in the setting of an upper respiratory tract infection with pharyngitis. She was hospitalized on [REDACTED](b)(6) and was treated with ceftazidime, fluconazole, and acyclovir. Pertinent laboratory values included absolute neutrophil count 0.81 g/L, hemoglobin 13.1 g/L, CRP 179 mg/L, fibrinogen 648 mg/dL (reference range 200-393 mg/dL). Peripheral smear showed a low percentage of segmented granulocytes (11%, reference range 34 to 71%), and myeloproliferative disease was excluded via cytomorphological examination. She was also found to have a "solid and fluid-filled lesion of 6x4mm on the right lobe of the thyroid." She was diagnosed with isolated neutropenia, "bone marrow damage," and pharyngitis. She was discharged on [REDACTED](b)(6). She had already completed blinded study treatment, as her last date of oral drug treatment was [REDACTED](b)(6). Concurrent TEAEs included gastroesophageal reflux disease and pharyngitis.
L800: This event was assessed as doubtfully related to study treatment by the investigator. On the last double-blind period study visit (Week 104; [REDACTED](b)(6)), this subject had values within the reference range for hemoglobin, lymphocytes, neutrophils, platelets, erythrocytes, and leukocytes. She initiated participation in Study TG1101-RMS303, with the first ublituximab-xiiy infusion on [REDACTED](b)(6). The closest study visit during participation in Study TG1101-RMS303 was the screening visit, on [REDACTED](b)(6), at which time this subject had values within the reference range for hemoglobin, lymphocytes, neutrophils, platelets, erythrocytes, and leukocytes.
L801: This event was assessed as doubtfully related to study treatment by the investigator. She consented to Study TG1101-RMS303 on [REDACTED](b)(6) and restarted ublituximab-xiiy [REDACTED](b)(6).
L802: **Reviewer comment:** The narrative indicates that the subject's neutrophil count was 0 in the setting of a presumed respiratory tract infection, approximately 1 year after the most recent dose of ublituximab-xiiy and prior to enrollment in the extension study. However, the ANC documented in the narrative is 0.81 \times 10^9/L, which is not consistent with agranulocytosis. It is not clear why this event was coded as bone marrow toxicity, as decreases in other cell lines were not noted. This event would likely have been more appropriately coded as neutropenia. Though neutropenia was reported in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy, this event appears to be potentially related to an acute infection rather than ublituximab-xiiy given the temporality. Of note, the neutrophil count mentioned in the narrative was not documented in the ADBL dataset for Study TG1101-RMS302 or TG1101-RMS303.

L807: Reviewer comment: Endometrial stromal sarcomas are rare malignant uterine tumors. This subject developed a low-grade tumor, which portends a better prognosis. Concomitant medications included an intrauterine contraceptive device. It is not clear why this SAE was rated as CTCAE Grade 2, as it is a severe, medically significant event that required hospitalization and surgical intervention. Based upon CTCAE definitions, this event should be considered at least Grade 3. See Section 8.3.1.

L831: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) (21 year-old man who received teriflunomide during double-blind treatment) experienced the SAEs febrile neutropenia, immune reconstitution inflammatory syndrome (IRIS), and interstitial lung disease during the extension period while receiving ublituximab-xiiy. L832: *Reviewer comment: The basis for a diagnosis of IRIS is not clear in this case. The event occurred &lt;1 month after initiating ublituximab-xiiy, after which immune reconstitution (i.e., B-cell repletion) would not be expected for at least 6 months. This reviewer suspects a viral pneumonia with associated neutropenia, which are likely related to ublituximab-xiiy.*

L844: The SAE colitis was reported in Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), which is considered of interest due to recent updates to labeling for ocrelizumab, another anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody. This subject was a 38 year-old woman who received ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6) (under Study TG1101-RMS201), then [REDACTED](b) (6). She presented on [REDACTED](D) (b) with abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, rectal bleeding, and bloody stools. CT scan showed "mild segmental wall thickening within the mid transverse colon possibly related to lack of distension." Laboratory evaluation was notable for positive fecal occult blood test, leukocytosis, and increased ANC. She was treated with antibiotics and symptomatic therapies (no steroids), and was discharged on [REDACTED](b) (6) with resolution of this event. No GI consult or colonoscopy were performed. No action was taken with ublituximab-xiiy during this event, and the subject continued study treatment as planned.
L845: *Reviewer comment: Though serious cases of colitis have been observed with ocrelizumab, this event does not appear to resemble such cases. Based on the nausea and vomiting observed with this case and resolution without immunotherapy (i.e., no steroids) or discontinuation of ublituximab-xiiy (i.e., negative rechallenge), this case is not likely to represent immune-mediated colitis due to ublituximab-xiiy. Given the spontaneous resolution, infectious colitis is thought to be more likely; however, no infectious studies were reported in the narrative.*

On [REDACTED](b) (6), she experienced Grade 2 myocardial ischemia, for which she was treated with metoprolol and rosuvastatin and the event resolved the same day. This event was considered an IRR by the investigator. Notably, her blood pressure was 90/60mmHg on the day of this event. Her heart rate was documented as 80 bpm. Pre-dose ECG was notable for incomplete right bundle branch block (RBBB, which was also observed at screening). Post-dose, she was noted to have ischemic changes (ST depression in II, III, AVF; T-wave inversion in V2-V5). Later that day, her ECG again demonstrated RBBB. ECGs on [REDACTED](b) (6) showed "ischemic type changes" and on [REDACTED](b) (6) showed incomplete RBBB. On [REDACTED](b) (6) her urinalysis was suggestive of infection, ALC was low at 0.4 \times 10^9/L, and ANC was high at 8.6 \times 10^9/L; a concurrent TEAE of lymphopenia was also reported. She discontinued study treatment due to the event of myocardial ischemia.
L863: *Reviewer comment: The etiology and characterization of this subject's "myocardial ischemia" is not clear from the information provided in the narrative. It is possible that the subject experienced hypotension (potentially due to a delayed infusion-related reaction), leading to demand ischemia and a positive troponin level, but this information is not provided. The role of her likely urinary tract infection in this event is also unclear.*
L864: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) is discussed in Section 8.4.2. She appeared to have recurrent IRRs which likely led to discontinuation, but the reason listed for discontinuation was "sponsor or investigator decision."

L870: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (a 41 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the SAE chronic hepatitis B (Grade 2, "chronic viral hepatitis B") on [REDACTED](b) (6). She received her first dose of ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED](b) (6), with subsequent infusions on [REDACTED](b) (6). Concurrent TEAEs included AST increased. She experienced AST 100 U/L and ALT 187 U/L on [REDACTED](b) (6), then AST 235 U/L and ALT 293 U/L on [REDACTED](b) (6). Hepatitis B surface antigen was positive. She was treated with ademetionine and tenofovir [REDACTED](b) (6) and discontinued study participation due to this AE. Her last study labs on [REDACTED](b) (6) were notable for ALT 18 U/L and AST 21 U/L (normal). At screening [REDACTED](b) (6), hepatitis B surface antigen was negative.
L871: Reviewer comment: Though hepatitis B surface antigen was negative at screening, this test alone is not adequate to determine whether a subject was previously infected with hepatitis B and therefore at risk for reactivation. Labeling for anti-CD20 therapies discusses hepatitis B reactivation risk and delineates the appropriate required screening; hepatitis B will be discussed in potential ublituximab-xiiy labeling as well.

L883: ## 8.4.4. Significant Adverse Events
L884: Significant adverse events are discussed in other sections of this safety review. However, a case of hepatotoxicity in an ublituximab-xiiy-treated subject warrants discussion.
L885: - Subject [REDACTED] (a 38 year-old woman receiving ublituximab-xiiy) experienced the AE hepatotoxicity (Grade 3) on [REDACTED] weeks after her second ublituximab-xiiy infusion. She began treatment with ublituximab-xiiy on [REDACTED], and received subsequent infusions on [REDACTED] Peak AST [REDACTED]
L886: was 229 U/L on [REDACTED](b)(6) normalized to 16 U/L on [REDACTED](b)(6), then was normal for the remainder of the study. Peak ALT was 388 U/L on [REDACTED](b)(6), normalized to 20 U/L on [REDACTED](b)(6), then was normal for the remainder of the study. Concurrent AEs included thrombocytopenia (platelet nadir 98 x 109/L, with recovery to baseline 1 week later), pyrexia, and GGT increased. Alkaline phosphatase was normal. Concomitant medications included propranolol and cholecalciferol. Additionally, per the Patient Profile document, this AE involved a protocol deviation, as the event occurred on the Day 28 visit but was not recognized until the Day 56 Visit by the investigators. Therefore, protocol procedures for this event were not followed and it does not appear that other evaluation was performed. The subject completed study participation as planned, and rechallenge was negative.
L887: *Reviewer comment: The etiology of this subject's AST/ALT elevation and thrombocytopenia is not clear, but the fact that the laboratory values resolved despite continued treatment suggests an etiology other than ublituximab-xiiy. However, a relationship to ublituximab-xiiy cannot be excluded based on the information provided.*

L926: ## Study TG1101-RMS201
L927: In Study TG1101-RMS201, 100% of subjects experienced at least 1 TEAE while on ublituximab-xiiy, compared to 61.5% of subjects while receiving placebo. TEAEs in the SOC Nervous System Disorders were most commonly reported (70.8% ublituximab-xiiy, 0% placebo). TEAEs in the SOC Infections and Infestations occurred in 58.3% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (compared to 30.8% when on placebo), and TEAEs under the SOC Gastrointestinal Disorders occurred in 58.3% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (compared to 7.7% when on placebo). TEAEs in all other SOCs occurred more frequently in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus placebo.
L928: TEAEs occurring in &gt;5% of subjects are listed by PT in Table 64. The most common TEAEs that were more frequent while subjects were receiving ublituximab-xiiy were fatigue (29.2%), headache (25.0%), nausea (18.8%), arthralgia (14.6%), dizziness (14.6%), hypoesthesia (14.6%), infusion related reaction (14.6%), and upper respiratory tract infection (14.6%).
L929: *Reviewer comment: The safety data from Study TG1101-RMS201 are not considered placebo-controlled due to the delayed start design.*

L939: ## 8.4.6. Laboratory Findings
L940: Laboratory data from ADBL were analyzed for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. This analysis will focus on laboratory parameters of interest that were identified based on prior knowledge of the safety profile of anti-CD20 therapies, as well as the adverse events from the ublituximab-xiiy development program.
L941: Laboratory values were evaluated at each scheduled timepoint (designated by applicant's ANL01FL=Y in ADBL dataset), and descriptive analyses (including analyses of change from baseline) are presented for Baseline, Week 4, Week 24, Week 48, and Week 96 of the DBT period for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. OLE data from Study TG1101-RMS303 were also reviewed for overall consistency with DBT period findings. Laboratory data collected via local laboratories or outside of scheduled study visits are discussed in the context of adverse events.

L951: Additionally, 20.5% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 56) and 29.8% of subjects on teriflunomide (n = 82) experienced WBCs between 2.0 to 3.5 \times 10^9/L, and 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 2) and 0.7% of subjects on teriflunomide (n = 2) experienced WBCs \le 2.0 \times 10^9/L.
L952: Reviewer comment: The peak in WBCs at Week 1 Day 2 (Figure 6) likely reflects reactive leukocytosis in response to corticosteroid administration in the setting of infusion pre-medication. Overall, there appears to be a trend towards lower WBC count in the teriflunomide group compared to ublituximab-xiiy, as mean and median WBC values appeared to be lower in teriflunomide-treated subjects, and the mean/median changes from baseline tended to be more negative in teriflunomide-treated subjects. This finding is not necessarily unexpected due to the broader lymphocyte targeting of teriflunomide compared to ublituximab-xiiy. Additionally, after Week 96 (when teriflunomide was stopped), WBC counts appeared to recover in this group. Week 116 values are difficult to interpret due to low sample size at that time point (n = 9 ublituximab-xiiy, n = 15 teriflunomide).
L953: Please refer to Section 8.5.4 for discussion of TEAEs related to leukocyte count (e.g., leukopenia).

L973: Additionally, 29.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 81) and 26.5% of subjects on teriflunomide (n = 73) experienced ALC between 0.5 to 1.0 \times 10^9/L, and 61.2% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 167) and 3.6% of subjects on teriflunomide (n = 10) experienced ALC \le 0.5 \times 10^9/L.
L974: *Reviewer comment: Subjects who received ublituximab-xiiy were much more likely to experience ALC \le 0.5 \times 10^9/L (61.2% ublituximab-xiiy versus 3.6% teriflunomide). Though low ALC is not unexpected given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy, this frequency of severe lymphopenia is concerning. This observation is likely driven by ALC at Week 1 Day 2, where the median ALC in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy decreased sharply then recovered, but remained lower than that of teriflunomide for most of the treatment period.*
L975: Please refer to Section 8.5.4 for discussion of TEAEs related to lymphocyte count (e.g., lymphopenia).
L976: ## *Hemoglobin, Hematocrit, and Erythrocytes*
L977: There did not appear to be a clinically significant difference or trend in hemoglobin, hematocrit, or erythrocyte count values or change between the treatment groups. The frequency of subjects experiencing abnormal hemoglobin (either &lt;120 g/L or &gt;160 g/L) or hematocrit (either &lt;0.35 L/L or &gt;0.5 L/L) was similar between the treatment groups. However, subjects on teriflunomide were more likely to experience erythrocytes &lt;4.0 \times 10^{12}/L compared to those on ublituximab-xiiy (20.0% versus 12.5%, respectively).

L985: Low platelet counts were more frequent in teriflunomide-treated subjects. Specifically, 14.3% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 39) and 19.3% of subjects on teriflunomide (n = 53) experienced a platelet count between 100 and 150 \times 10^9/L, and 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 2) and 1.5% of subjects on teriflunomide (n = 4) experienced a platelet count between 50 and 100 \times 10^9/L. No subjects experienced a platelet count &lt;50 \times 10^9/L.
L986: *Reviewer comment: Teriflunomide-treated subjects tended to have a lower platelet count than those on ublituximab-xiiy post-baseline. A 10% reduction in platelet count was observed in subjects on teriflunomide compared to placebo in pivotal trials supporting approval of teriflunomide, and reduced platelets are described as a labeled adverse event for teriflunomide, so this is not unexpected. However, a few patients on ublituximab-xiiy experienced a platelet count &lt;100 \times 10^9/L. The etiology of these cases is not clear; we will continue postmarketing surveillance for additional cases of thrombocytopenia. Thrombocytopenia is not an established risk of anti-CD20 therapies. Moreover, anti-CD20 therapies are sometimes used for treatment of immune-mediated thrombocytopenia.*
L987: Please refer to Section 8.5.4 for discussion of TEAEs related to platelet count (e.g., thrombocytopenia).

L996: Subjects with significant elevations in AST are discussed in Section 8.5.5.
L997: Shift analyses indicated that 5.5% of patients on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 15) and 1.8% of patients on teriflunomide (n = 5) experienced increased AST to &gt;3x ULN from baseline values of &lt;3x ULN.
L998: *Reviewer comment: Comparison of summary statistics (e.g., mean/median AST values and change from baseline) did not indicate a clinically meaningful difference in AST over time between the groups. However, elevations in AST were more common in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to teriflunomide. It is important to note that hepatotoxicity is a known safety issue with teriflunomide so subjects experiencing an increase in AST in the ublituximab-xiiy group twice the rate for a comparator with a boxed warning for hepatotoxicity is an unanticipated finding. Refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion regarding the DILI team consultation obtained for this application.*
L999: Please refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion of TEAEs related to hepatotoxicity and further shift analyses across Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.

L1008: Subjects with significant elevations in ALT are discussed in Section 8.5.5.
L1009: Shift analyses indicated that 3.3% of patients on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 9) and 3.3% of patients on teriflunomide (n = 9) experienced increased ALT to &gt;3x ULN from baseline values of &lt;3x ULN. Elevations in ALT above the ULN occurred at a similar frequency in the ublituximab-xiiy (4.0%) and teriflunomide groups (3.3%).
L1010: *Reviewer comment: Comparison of summary statistics (e.g., mean/median ALT and change in ALT from baseline) did not indicate a clinically meaningful difference in ALT over time between the groups. However, it is important to note that hepatotoxicity is a known safety issue with teriflunomide. Refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion regarding the DILI team consultation obtained for this application.*
L1011: Please refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion of TEAEs related to hepatotoxicity and further shift analyses across Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.
L1012: ## Bilirubin
L1013: Total, direct, and indirect bilirubin concentrations (in \mu\text{mol/L}) were reviewed, and there were no apparent clinically significant differences between the treatment groups in terms of mean/median values, peak values, or changes from baseline.
L1014: Elevations in total bilirubin above the ULN were more commonly observed in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy (1.5%) compared to those on teriflunomide (0.4%). However, a similar percentage of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (2.6%) and teriflunomide (2.5%) experienced a shift from a normal baseline total bilirubin (&lt;1.5\times ULN) to a high total bilirubin (&gt;1.5\times ULN).
L1015: *Reviewer comment: The frequency of bilirubin elevations appears higher in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy compared to those on teriflunomide.*
L1016: Please refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion of TEAEs related to hepatotoxicity and further shift analyses across Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.
L1017: ## *Hy's Law Cases*
L1018: One potential Hy's Law case (AST or ALT &gt;3\times ULN, total bilirubin &gt;2\times ULN, and ALP&lt;2\times ULN) was reported in a subject receiving ublituximab-xiiy. This subject (ID # [REDACTED](b)(6)) experienced a TEAE with the PT hepatitis toxic (Grade 2) that led to treatment discontinuation, and is discussed in Section 8.4.3.
L1019: No potential Hy's Law cases were noted in the teriflunomide group.
L1020: Please refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion of TEAEs related to hepatotoxicity and further shift analyses across Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302.
L1021: ## *Alkaline Phosphatase*
L1022: Alkaline phosphatase (IU/L) values over time were reviewed, and there were no apparent clinically significant differences between the treatment groups in terms of mean/median values, peak values, or changes from baseline.
L1023: Elevations in ALP &gt;1.5\times ULN from a normal baseline (&lt;1.5\times ULN) occurred in 0.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 1.1% on teriflunomide. Additionally, 2 subjects on teriflunomide (0.7%) experienced peak ALP between 2 and 3\times ULN, compared to 0 subjects on ublituximab-xiiy.
L1024: ## *Renal Function*
L1025: There were no apparent clinically significant trends for blood urea nitrogen (BUN) or creatinine in either treatment group over time, as mean/median values and changes from baseline were similar between the treatment groups. Additionally, creatinine values \ge 130 \mu\text{mol/L} and BUN values &gt;9 \text{mmol/L} occurred at similar frequencies in both treatment groups.
L1026: ## *Pancreatic Enzymes*
L1027: Amylase elevations 2 to 3x ULN were reported in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, and 0.4% on teriflunomide; elevations 5 to 10x ULN were reported in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 0% on teriflunomide. Lipase elevations 3 to 5x ULN were reported in 1.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 2.2% on teriflunomide; elevations 5 to 10x ULN were reported in 0.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 1.8% on teriflunomide.
L1028: Amylase &gt;400 U/L occurred in 2 subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy, both of whom were reported as having TEAEs. One of these subjects (# [REDACTED](b)(6)) was the 32 year-old woman who experienced measles and encephalitis leading to death (Section 8.4.1).
L1029: Lipase &gt;400 U/L occurred in 1 subject receiving ublituximab-xiiy ([REDACTED](b)(6)) at Week 8, and was reported as a TEAE.
L1030: TEAEs of amylase increased were reported in 2.2% (n = 6) of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0.4% (n = 1) on teriflunomide. TEAEs of lipase increased were reported in 2.6% (n = 7) of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 4.7% (n = 13) on teriflunomide. Pancreatitis chronic was reported in 0.4% (n = 1) subject on ublituximab-xiiy versus 1.1% (n = 3) on teriflunomide. TEAEs of pancreatic toxicity was reported in 0.7% (n = 2) of subjects on teriflunomide, but none in ublituximab-xiiy. The TEAE pancreatic steatosis was reported in 0.4% (n = 1) of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, but none in teriflunomide.
L1031: *Reviewer comment: Of note, teriflunomide labeling contains a warning for pancreatitis in pediatric patients and a separate mention of pancreatitis as an adverse reaction identified in postmarketing experience. Though there is no obvious signal for pancreatic toxicity at this time, a concern was raised during the review of ocrelizumab and enhanced pharmacovigilance was recommended. Enhanced pharmacovigilance with ublituximab-xiiy also appears to be warranted based on these data as well.*
L1032: ## *Other Chemistry*
L1033: There were no apparent clinically significant trends for urate, albumin, sodium, calcium, phosphate, and chloride in either treatment group over time, as mean/median values and changes from baseline were similar between the treatment groups.
L1034: ## Immunoglobulins
L1035: ## *IgG*
L1036: Median IgG for the ublituximab-xiiy group was 9.7 g/L at baseline, 9.8 g/L at week 24, then 10.0
L1037: g/L at week 96, compared to 9.5 g/L, 8.7 g/L, and 9.9 g/L for teriflunomide, respectively. Across the DBT period, 1.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 5) and 3.3% on teriflunomide (n = 9) experienced IgG &lt;5.5 g/L. Median change in IgG from baseline over the DBT period is presented in Figure 12.
L1038: Figure 12 (Reviewer). Study TG1101-RMS301, Median Change in IgG (g/L) from Baseline Over Time, Safety Population
L1039: Error bars represent interquartile range; Source: ADBL, SAFFL=Y, ANL01FL=Y, PARAMCD=IGG, by TRT01A
L1040: Reviewer comment: Though expected long-term given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy, there was not a clear trend for reduction in IgG associated with ublituximab-xiiy during the DBT period. Median values at Week 116 should be interpreted with caution due to the small number of subjects at that time (n = 9 on ublituximab-xiiy, 15 on teriflunomide). Changes in IgG noted for ocrelizumab required years of exposure to identify, so the lack of a finding in the DBT period was expected and does not preclude labeling for what appears to be an effect that requires prolonged exposure to any anti-CD20 therapy.
L1041: ## IgM
L1042: Median IgM for the ublituximab-xiiy group was 1.4 g/L at baseline, 1.0 g/L at week 24, then 0.9 g/L at week 96, compared to 1.4 g/L, 1.1 g/L, and 1.2 g/L for teriflunomide, respectively. Across the DBT period, 20.5% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 56) and 5.5% on teriflunomide (n = 15) experienced IgM &lt;0.4 g/L. Median change in IgM from baseline over the DBT period is presented in Figure 13.

Additionally, after Week 96 (when teriflunomide was stopped), WBC counts appeared to recover in this group; week 116 values are difficult to interpret due to low sample size at that time point (n = 3 ublituximab-xiiy, n = 8 teriflunomide). However, in contrast to Study TG1101-RMS301 results, subjects on ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to have WBCs \le 2.0 \times 10^9/L compared to those on teriflunomide.

L1086: *Reviewer comment: Similar to the results of Study TG1101-RMS301, subjects who received ublituximab-xiiy were much more likely to experience ALC \le 0.5 \times 10^9/L (55.1% ublituximab-xiiy versus 3.3% teriflunomide). Though low ALC is not unexpected given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy, this frequency of severe lymphopenia is concerning. This observation is likely driven by ALC at Week 1 Day 2, where the median ALC in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy decreased sharply then recovered, but remained lower than that of teriflunomide through Week 12.*
L1087: Please refer to Section 8.5.4 for discussion of TEAEs related to lymphocyte count (e.g., lymphopenia).
L1088: ## *Hemoglobin, Hematocrit, and Erythrocytes*
L1089: There did not appear to be a clinically significant difference or trend in hemoglobin, hematocrit,
L1090: or erythrocyte count values or change between the treatment groups. The frequency of subjects experiencing abnormal hemoglobin (either &lt;120 g/L or &gt;160 g/L) or hematocrit (either &lt;0.35 L/L or &gt;0.5 L/L) was similar between the treatment groups. However, subjects on teriflunomide were more likely to experience erythrocyte count &lt;4.0 \times 10^{12}/L compared to those on ublituximab-xiiy (18.7% versus 12.9%, respectively).

A 10% reduction in platelet count was observed in subjects on teriflunomide compared to placebo in pivotal trials, so this is not unexpected. However, a few patients on ublituximab-xiiy experienced a platelet count &lt;100 \times 10^9/L. See reviewer comment regarding this finding in Study TG1101-RMS301.*
L1100: Please refer to Section 8.5.4 for discussion of TEAEs related to platelet count (e.g., thrombocytopenia).

L1125: ## Bilirubin
L1126: Total, direct, and indirect bilirubin concentrations (in \mu\text{mol/L}) were reviewed, and there were no apparent clinically significant differences between the treatment groups in terms of mean/median values, peak values, or changes from baseline.
L1127: Elevations in total bilirubin above the ULN occurred at a similar frequency in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy (1.1%) and teriflunomide (1.1%). A similar percentage of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (2.2%) and teriflunomide (2.6%) experienced a shift from a normal baseline total bilirubin (&lt;1.5\times ULN) to a high total bilirubin (&gt;1.5\times ULN).
L1128: *Reviewer comment: The frequency of bilirubin elevations appears similar in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy and teriflunomide in Study TG1101-RMS302. However, one subject on ublituximab-xiiy experienced total bilirubin 5 to 10x ULN, compared to none on teriflunomide.*
L1129: Please refer to Section 8.5.5 for discussion of TEAEs related to hepatotoxicity.
L1130: ## *Hy's Law Cases*
L1131: One subject on ublituximab-xiiy (0.4%) and 2 on teriflunomide (0.7%) were considered as potential Hy's Law cases (AST or ALT &gt;3\times ULN, total bilirubin &gt;2\times ULN, and ALP&lt;2\times ULN). The subject on ublituximab-xiiy is ID # [REDACTED](b) (6), who was a 46 year-old woman who experienced the SAE chronic hepatitis B. This case is discussed in Section 8.4.2.
L1132: *Reviewer comment: The subject on ublituximab-xiiy who met Hy's Law Criteria did so in the setting of Hepatitis B, and is therefore not representative of drug-induced liver injury. The 2 cases reported in teriflunomide-treated subjects are not unexpected given the safety profile of teriflunomide. See Section 8.5.5.*
L1133: ## *Alkaline phosphatase*
L1134: Alkaline phosphatase (IU/L) values over time were reviewed, and there were no apparent clinically significant differences between the treatment groups in terms of mean/median values, peak values, or changes from baseline.
L1135: Elevations in ALP &gt;1.5\times ULN from a normal baseline (&lt;1.5\times ULN) occurred in 0.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 0.7% on teriflunomide. Additionally, 1 subject on teriflunomide (0.4%) experienced peak ALP between 2 and 3\times ULN, compared to 0 subjects on ublituximab-xiiy.
L1136: ## *Renal function*
L1137: There were no apparent clinically significant trends for BUN or creatinine in either treatment group over time, as mean/median values and changes from baseline were similar between the treatment groups. Creatinine values \ge 130 \mu\text{mol/L} were slightly more frequent in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects (0.7% ublituximab-xiiy versus 0.4% teriflunomide), but BUN values &gt;9 \text{mmol/L} were more common in teriflunomide-treated subjects (5.9% teriflunomide versus 1.5%
L1138: ublituximab-xiiy). However, maximum BUN and creatinine values were similar between the groups.
L1139: ## *Pancreatic Enzymes*
L1140: Amylase elevations 2 to 3x ULN were reported in 0.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, and 0% on teriflunomide; elevations 3 to 5x ULN were reported in 0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 0.4% on teriflunomide. Lipase elevations 3 to 5x ULN were reported in 0.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 2.6% on teriflunomide; elevations 5 to 10x ULN were reported in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 0.7% on teriflunomide.
L1141: No subjects in either treatment group experienced amylase &gt;400 U/L. Lipase &gt;400 U/L occurred in 2 subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy (# [REDACTED](b)(6)). The elevation in subject [REDACTED](b)(6) occurred on Day 2 of the study. Both subjects had these values documented as TEAEs.
L1142: TEAEs of amylase increased were reported in 1.8% (n = 5) of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0% on teriflunomide. TEAEs of lipase increased were reported in 2.9% (n = 8) of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 2.2% (n = 6) on teriflunomide. TEAEs of pancreatic enzymes increased were reported in 0.7% (n = 2) of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0% on teriflunomide. Pancreatic disorder was reported in 0.4% (n = 1) subject on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0% on teriflunomide. Pancreatitis haemorrhagic was reported in 0.4% (n = 1) subject on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0% on teriflunomide. The TEAEs pancreatic toxicity and pancreatitis acute were reported in 0.4% (n = 1) of subjects on teriflunomide, but none in ublituximab-xiiy. The TEAE pancreatic steatosis was reported in 0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, and 0.4% (n = 1) of subjects on teriflunomide.
L1143: *Reviewer comment: Of note, teriflunomide labeling contains a warning for pancreatitis in pediatric patients and a separate mention of pancreatitis as an adverse reaction identified in postmarketing experience. Though there is no obvious signal for pancreatic toxicity at this time, a concern was raised during the review of ocrelizumab and enhanced pharmacovigilance was recommended. Enhanced pharmacovigilance also appears to be warranted based on these data as well.*
L1144: ## *Other Chemistry*
L1145: There were no apparent clinically significant trends for urate, albumin, sodium, calcium, phosphate, and chloride in either treatment group over time, as mean/median values and changes from baseline were similar between the treatment groups.
L1146: ## Immunoglobulins
L1147: ## *IgG*
L1148: Median IgG for the ublituximab-xiiy group was 9.9 g/L at baseline, 9.9 g/L at week 24, then 10.0 g/L at week 96, compared to 9.5 g/L, 8.9 g/L, and 9.9 g/L for teriflunomide, respectively. Across the DBT period, 2.6% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 7) and 1.8% on teriflunomide (n = 5) experienced IgG &lt;5.5 g/L. Median change in IgG from baseline over the DBT period is presented in Figure 21.

Median values after Week 96 should be interpreted with caution due to the small number of subjects with recorded values at those time points.*
L1152: ## IgM
L1153: Median IgM for the ublituximab-xiiy group was 1.3 g/L at baseline, 1.0 g/L at week 24, then 0.7 g/L at week 96, compared to 1.4 g/L, 1.1 g/L, and 1.2 g/L for teriflunomide, respectively. Across the DBT period, 23.9% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy (n = 65) and 6.6% on teriflunomide (n = 18) experienced IgM &lt;0.4 g/L. L1156: *Reviewer comment: Reduction in immunoglobulins is expected long-term given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy, and there was a clear trend for declining IgM levels over time in the ublituximab-xiiy group compared to teriflunomide over at least 96 weeks of DBT. Sample size at Weeks 100, 104, and 116 was small and therefore these values should be interpreted with caution.*

L1165: *Reviewer comment: It appears that ublituximab-xiiy depleted CD19+ B-cells in a majority of subjects. Several subjects did not have complete B-cell depletion, and one subject who had 3 relapses also had incomplete B-cell depletion.*
L1166: ## Study TG1101-RMS303
L1167: Of note, only tabulation datasets (i.e., SDTM) were submitted for Study TG1101-RMS303; no analysis datasets (ADAM) were submitted. The SDTM laboratory dataset submitted only contained screening and Week 1 Day 1 laboratory values for IgG and IgM. Therefore, analysis of immunoglobulins in the setting of longer-term exposure was planned but not conducted.
L1168: ## Study TG1101-RMS201
L1169: Given the size, duration, and supportive nature of Study TG1101-RMS201, only B-cell counts
L1170: will be reviewed. As discussed in Section 6.4, the primary endpoint for Study TG1101-RMS201 was the percentage of subjects who experienced a &gt;95% reduction in CD19+ B-cells. However, the applicant conducted this analysis using the percentage of CD19+ B-cells, rather than the absolute count, which is not interpretable. Additionally, the absolute CD19+ B-cell count was not collected after screening, so it is not possible to conduct such analyses in this trial population.
L1171: ## 8.4.7. Vital Signs
L1172: Vital signs, specifically blood pressure (systolic and diastolic), heart rate, temperature, and weight, were reviewed at each scheduled study visit (i.e., ANL01FL = Y) for the safety population. Vital sign-related TEAEs are also discussed in this section.
L1173: ## Study TG1101-RMS301
L1174: ## Blood Pressure
L1175: Systolic blood pressure (SBP) below 90 mmHg or above 160 mmHg was infrequent in both treatment groups. SBP &lt;90 mmHg occurred in 1.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.7% on teriflunomide. SBP &gt;160 mmHg occurred in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.5% on teriflunomide.
L1176: Diastolic blood pressure (DBP) below 60 mmHg occurred in 5.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 5.1% on teriflunomide, indicating similar frequencies. DBP &gt;90 mmHg occurred in 16.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 22.9% on teriflunomide.
L1177: The TEAE hypertension occurred in 4.0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 8.0% on teriflunomide. The TEAE blood pressure increased occurred in 1.5% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 1.8% on teriflunomide. Hypertensive crisis occurred in 1 subject on teriflunomide (0.4%). Hypotension occurred in 1.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0% on teriflunomide.
L1178: *Reviewer comment: Teriflunomide labeling currently contains a warning regarding increased blood pressure. TEAEs related to increased blood pressure occurred at a higher frequency in the teriflunomide group.*
L1179: ## Heart Rate
L1180: Heart rate (HR) below 50 beats per minute (bpm) or above 120 bpm was infrequent in both treatment groups. HR &lt;50 bpm occurred in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.5% on teriflunomide. No subjects experienced HR &gt;120bpm at a scheduled study visit.
L1181: The TEAE tachycardia was reported in 3.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, compared to 2.2% on teriflunomide. The TEAE sinus tachycardia was reported in 1.8% of subjects in each group. Heart rate increased was reported in 0.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0% on teriflunomide.
L1182: The TEAEs bradycardia and sinus bradycardia each were reported in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide. Heart rate-related TEAEs are also summarized in Table 79.
L1183: ## Weight
L1184: Similar proportions of subjects in each treatment group experienced a &gt;10% weight loss compared to baseline (13.9% ublituximab-xiiy versus 14.9% teriflunomide). However, subjects in the ublituximab-xiiy group were more likely to experience a &gt;10% weight gain compared to baseline (14.7% ublituximab-xiiy versus 9.8% teriflunomide).
L1185: The TEAE weight increased occurred in 1.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.7% on teriflunomide. The TEAE weight decreased occurred in 1.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 3.3% on teriflunomide.
L1186: ## Temperature
L1187: No subjects in either group experienced temperature &gt;38.1°C at a scheduled study visit. Similar proportions of subjects in each group experienced temperature &lt;36.0°C at a scheduled study visit (14.3% ublituximab-xiiy versus 14.5% teriflunomide).
L1188: The TEAE pyrexia occurred in 15.0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 4.7% on teriflunomide. The TEAE body temperature increased occurred in 4.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.1% on teriflunomide. The TEAE hyperthermia occurred in 4.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.7% on teriflunomide.
L1189: *Reviewer comment: Though the frequency of abnormal temperature at scheduled study visits was similar between the groups, subjects on ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience TEAEs of pyrexia, body temperature increased, or hyperthermia. This is not unexpected given reports of these events as IRRs.*
L1190: Study TG1101-RMS302
L1191: ## Blood Pressure
L1192: Systolic blood pressure (SBP) below 90 mmHg or above 160 mmHg was infrequent in both
L1193: treatment groups. SBP &lt;90 mmHg was more frequent in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects (1.5% ublituximab-xiiy versus 0.4% teriflunomide). SBP &gt;160 mmHg occurred in 1.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.8% on teriflunomide.
L1194: Diastolic blood pressure (DBP) below 60 mmHg occurred in 5.9% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 2.6% on teriflunomide. DBP &gt;90 mmHg occurred in 16.5% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 21.2% on teriflunomide.
L1195: The TEAE hypertension occurred in 3.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 5.5% on teriflunomide. The TEAE blood pressure increased occurred in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 0.7% on teriflunomide. Blood pressure systolic increased occurred in 0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide, and essential hypertension was reported in 0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide.
L1196: Hypotension occurred in 0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.1% on teriflunomide, and blood pressure decreased occurred in 0% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide.
L1197: *Reviewer comment: Teriflunomide labeling currently contains a warning regarding increased blood pressure. DBP elevation &gt;90 mmHg was more frequent in teriflunomide-treated subjects, but SBP &gt;160 mmHg occurred at a similar frequency in each group. TEAEs related to increased blood pressure appeared to be more frequent in the teriflunomide group as well.*
L1198: ## Heart Rate
L1199: HR below 50 beats per minute (bpm) or above 120 bpm was infrequent in both treatment groups. HR &lt;50 bpm occurred in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.8% on teriflunomide. One subject in the ublituximab-xiiy group (0.4%) experienced HR &gt;120bpm, compared to 0 in the teriflunomide group.
L1200: The TEAE sinus tachycardia was reported in 7.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, compared to 1.8% on teriflunomide. The TEAE tachycardia was reported in 3.7% of subjects in each group. Heart rate increased was reported in 1.5% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy versus 0.7% on teriflunomide.
L1201: The TEAE sinus bradycardia was reported in 1.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide. Sinus bradycardia was reported in 0.4% of subjects in each group.
L1202: Heart rate-related TEAEs are also summarized in Table 79.
L1203: ## Weight
L1204: Subjects on teriflunomide were slightly more likely to experience a &gt;10% weight loss compared to baseline compared to those on ublituximab-xiiy (12.8% teriflunomide versus 7.0% ublituximab-xiiy). Subjects in the ublituximab-xiiy group were more likely to experience a &gt;10% weight gain compared to baseline (17.3% ublituximab-xiiy versus 8.8% teriflunomide).
L1205: The TEAE weight increased occurred in 0.7% of subjects in both groups. The TEAE weight decreased occurred in 0.7% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide.
L1206: *Reviewer comment: The occurrence of weight-related TEAEs was not as frequent in Study TG1101-RMS302, and was similar between the treatment groups (for both weight loss and gain). However, subjects on ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience a &gt;10% weight gain.*
L1207: ## Temperature
L1208: Body temperature &gt;38.1°C at a scheduled study visit was infrequent in both groups, but occurred in 1.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.4% on teriflunomide. Similar proportions of subjects in each group experienced temperature &lt;36.0°C at a scheduled study visit (17.3% ublituximab-xiiy versus 21.6% teriflunomide).
L1209: The TEAE pyrexia occurred in 12.9% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 5.1% on teriflunomide. The TEAE hyperthermia occurred in 6.6% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.5% on teriflunomide. The TEAE body temperature increased occurred in 4.4% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 1.1% on teriflunomide.
L1210: *Reviewer comment: Though the frequency of abnormal temperature at scheduled study visits was similar between the groups, subjects on ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience TEAEs of pyrexia, body temperature increased, or hyperthermia. This observation is not surprising given the association of these PTs with IRRs.*
L1211: ## Study TG1101-RMS303
L1212: Given the open-label nature of Study TG1101-RMS303, detailed review of vital signs will not be presented.
L1213: ## Study TG1101-RMS201
L1214: Given the size, duration, and supportive nature of Study TG1101-RMS201, detailed review of vital signs will not be presented.
