L1221: ## 8.4.9. QT
L1222: ## Study TG1101-RMS301
L1223: Per the Summary of Clinical Safety, the applicant only performed normality interpretations for ECGs in Study TG1101-RMS301. QT values were not collected. The TEAE electrocardiogram QT prolonged was reported in 1.1% of subjects in each treatment group.
L1224: ## Study TG1101-RMS302
L1225: Per the Summary of Clinical Safety, the applicant only performed normality interpretations for ECGs in Study TG1101-RMS302. QT values were not collected. The TEAE electrocardiogram QT prolonged was reported in 1.1% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy and 0.7% on teriflunomide.
L1226: ## Study TG1101-RMS201
L1227: The applicant provided a tabulation of subjects with post-treatment QTcF &gt;450, &gt;480, and &gt;500 msec. All subjects had QTcF \le 450 msec at baseline. Post-baseline, 97.9% of subjects had QTcF \le 450 msec, and one subject (2.1%) had QTcF between 450 and 480 msec. Per the applicant's analysis, no subjects had a change from baseline &gt; 60msec. Change from baseline was \le30 msec in 91.7% of subjects and 30 to 60 msec in 8.3%.
L1228: *Reviewer comment: Overall, there does not appear to be a clinically significant effect of ublituximab-xiiy on QTc, as expected for a monoclonal antibody.*
L1229: ## 8.4.10. Immunogenicity
L1230: According to the applicant's Bioanalytical Report referenced in the CSR, 234 out of 271 (86.3%) of subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy in Study TG1101-RMS301 and 239 out of 272 (87.9%) subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy in Study TG1101-RMS302 developed anti-drug antibodies. Please refer to the Clinical Pharmacology Review.
L1231: One subject in Study TG1101-RMS301 (Subject [REDACTED](b) (6)) experienced IRRs with several ublituximab-xiiy infusions, and was found to have binding anti-drug antibodies (See Section 8.4.2, SAE of infusion related reaction). Screening confirmation testing for binding anti-drug antibodies was negative (after a presumptive positive screening), then on Weeks 3 and 24, confirmation testing was positive. Additionally, neutralizing antibody testing was positive at Week 24, after being negative at Weeks 1 and 3. She discontinued study treatment due to "investigator or sponsor decision," but it appears that recurrent IRRs in the setting of antidrug binding antibodies were the reason for discontinuation.
L1232: ## 8.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues

L1243: The death reported in an ocrelizumab-treated subject with RMS was due to suicide and therefore not drug-related. The deaths in interferon beta-1a-treated subjects in the ocrelizumab development program were due to suicide and ileus and were not considered to be drug-related.
L1244: Per the Clinical BLA Review for ocrelizumab by Dr. Gerard Boehm, in Study WA25046 for primary progressive MS, the mortality risk for ocrelizumab was 0.8% (4/486) compared to 0.4% (1/239) for placebo. The causes of death for ocrelizumab-treated subjects were pneumonia, pulmonary embolism, aspiration pneumonia, and metastatic pancreatic cancer.
L1245: *Reviewer comment: Though data from the PPMS population can be considered useful for comparison purposes, their generalizability to an RMS population is limited due to important differences in these populations, mainly older age and increased disability in the PPMS population and therefore increased susceptibility to infection and sequelae.*
L1246: Per the Clinical sBLA Review for ofatumumab by Dr. Lawrence Rodichok, in Study COMB157G2302 for RMS, one death occurred in a subject treated with teriflunomide. This subject experienced aortic and vertebral artery dissection 152 days after the last dose of teriflunomide, for which he or she underwent cardiothoracic surgery that was complicated by aortic rupture leading to death.
L1247: *Reviewer comment: The risk of mortality associated with serious infections in subjects treated with ublituximab-xiiy appears higher than that observed in the ofatumumab and ocrelizumab development programs. It is difficult to compare deaths attributed to COVID-19 in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy due to the fact that controlled trials for other anti-CD20 therapies were not occurring during the COVID-19 pandemic. Emerging literature during the COVID-19 pandemic has suggested a higher risk of severe COVID-19 disease and mortality associated with anti-CD20 therapies.*

L1254: Serious infections that occurred in the development programs for ocrelizumab and ofatumumab for RMS were also reviewed (Table 83). The pivotal trials for ofatumumab also utilized teriflunomide as a comparator, so are more appropriate for comparison with the ublituximab-xiiy development program. Though the frequency of infection-related SAEs is higher in both treatment groups in the ublituximab-xiiy development program compared to ofatumumab, the relative risk for an infection-related SAE for ublituximab-xiiy compared with teriflunomide is 1.7 (5.0%/2.9%), and for ofatumumab compared with teriflunomide is 1.4 (2.5%/1.8%). Infection-related SAEs were more frequent in interferon beta-1a-treated subjects compared to those on ocrelizumab in the pooled controlled phase 3 RMS trials; therefore, this comparison was not considered to be appropriate.

L1258: Four cases of central nervous system infections were reported in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy during the double-blind period of Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, and additional 3 cases have been reported in subjects during Study TG1101-RMS303 as of 7/5/2022. These events have included CNS enteroviral infection, encephalitis, meningoencephalitis viral, meningitis, meningitis enteroviral, and meningitis viral. Of note, a case of West Nile Virus meningoencephalitis in a 34 year-old woman in Study TG1101-RMS303 occurred on 8/27/2022 and was submitted to IND 127265 (Report #TGT002657).
L1259: PML was considered in 2 cases during the double-blind period, but in one case CSF JCV PCR was negative (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6)). In the other case, CSF JCV PCR was not reportedly available (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6)) so it is not possible to rule out PML, but the reported CSF profile appeared atypical for PML. An Information Request was sent on 7/28/2022, and a response was received on 8/12/2022, regarding cases with a potential diagnosis of PML. The applicant indicated that the MRI for Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was reviewed by an independent radiologist and was considered inconsistent with PML. For Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), the MRI findings were considered consistent with MS; PML was excluded based on high CSF pleocytosis, MRI findings, clinical features, and positive response to acyclovir. The applicant identified a teriflunomide-treated subject where the diagnosis of PML was considered (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6)); this subject was determined not to have PML based upon MRI findings.
L1260: The cases of viral meningitis, specifically enterovirus, were only observed in the ublituximab-xiiy group. A signal for viral meningitis has not been noted in clinical trials for other anti-CD20 therapies. Specifically, no cases of viral meningitis, encephalitis, or enteroviral infections were reported in ofatumumab or ocrelizumab controlled trials for RMS. In trials of ocrelizumab for other indications, one case of cryptococcal meningoencephalitis (subject with systemic lupus erythematosus/lupus nephritis) and one case of viral meningoencephalitis (subject with rheumatoid arthritis) were reported in subjects on ocrelizumab.
L1261: Of note, no serious herpes virus infections were reported in the ublituximab-xiiy development program. As of 7/5/22, per the applicant's analysis, herpes virus related infections were reported in 4.0% of ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects across all RMS studies.
L1262: COVID-19 infections are discussed in Section 8.5.7.
L1263: *Reviewer Comment: Though the relative risk of serious infections appear to be similar to that of ofatumumab compared to teriflunomide, the reports of viral meningitis and encephalitis in the ublituximab-xiiy development program are concerning and appear to be unique to ublituximab-xiiy.*
L1264: ## 8.5.3. Infusion-Related Reactions
L1265: Infusion-related reactions are a known risk associated with monoclonal antibodies, and were included as an AESI for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. TEAEs designated as IRRs by the investigators were reviewed. The applicant flagged IRRs in the ADAE dataset via 2 different variables: one indicating events that were considered by investigator to be an IRR (variable *IRR*) and the other using an algorithm-generated AESI flag using a custom query (variable *CQ02NAM*). CQ02NAM defined an IRR as an adverse event of a preferred term of
L1266: interest that occurred within 24 hours of an infusion, and was listed as recovered/resolved. The summary tabulations evaluating IRR below are based upon the investigator determination.
L1267: *Reviewer Comment: The Investigator-determined IRR flag was thought to be more appropriate and clinically relevant given the input of clinical judgment and lack of assumptions regarding occurrence and resolution within 24 hours.*
L1268: The Phase 3 study protocols for ublituximab-xiiy required premedication 30-60 minutes prior to each infusion with an oral antihistamine (diphenhydramine 50mg) and an oral corticosteroid (dexamethasone 10-20mg). Additionally, the protocol specified management guidelines for IRRs.
L1269: The applicant tabulated the number of infusions completed with and without interruption, as well as the number of incomplete infusions for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. These results, along with other pertinent data discussed in the applicant's CSR, are presented in Table 84. Infusion interruption was more common in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy versus teriflunomide (13.2% and 11.0% versus 2.2% and 1.8%, respectively).

L1273: SAEs considered to be infusion-related reactions are discussed in Section 8.4.2. These 4 events in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects (0.7%) included anaphylactic reaction (n = 1), infusion related reaction (n = 1; described as hand, arm, jaw, and leg pain), hypersensitivity (n = 1), and lymphocyte count decreased (n = 1). The events of hypersensitivity and lymphocyte count decreased occurred after the first infusion, anaphylactic reaction after the second infusion, and infusion related reaction after the fourth infusion of ublituximab-xiiy.
L1274: The overall frequency of TEAEs considered as IRRs by the investigator occurring in &gt;5 subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy in the pooled Study TG1101-RMS301 or TG1101-RMS302 population are reported in Table 85. IRRs occurred in 47.7% of subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy compared to 12.2% on teriflunomide. CTCAE Grade 3 or higher IRRs occurred in 2.8% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy compared to 0.2% on teriflunomide. The most common preferred terms for IRRs among ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects included pyrexia (9.5%), chills (7.9%), headache (7.5%), influenza-like illness (5.9%), infusion related reaction (5.0%), and hyperthermia (4.6%).
L1275: Table 85 also summarizes the frequency of IRRs by infusion number. Among subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, IRRs were most common with the first infusion (43.3% of subjects on ublituximab-xiiy) and decreased with subsequent infusions to 9.4% (Week 2), 7.9% (Week 24), 6.6% (Week 48) and 4.8% (Week 96).

L1279: Reviewer comment: Infusion-related reactions were frequent in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects, particularly with the first infusion, despite required pre-medication. These reactions were more likely to occur with the first infusion, with decreasing frequency over subsequent infusions. The case of anaphylaxis occurred with the second infusion. Pre-medication for prevention of infusion-related reactions will be included in labeling. The reported rates of IRR do not indicate a clear reduction over time to no risk, and therefore labeling should describe a need for monitoring for IRR after every infusion.
L1280: ## 8.5.4. Cytopenias
L1281: Cytopenias were also listed as an AESI for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. TEAEs designated as cytopenia-related AESIs by the applicant, as well as other TEAEs considered to be cytopenia-related were reviewed. Laboratory results are reviewed in Section 8.4.6.
L1282: Labeling for teriflunomide, the active comparator for these studies, contains a warning regarding Bone Marrow Effects, which mentions decreased leukocytes, neutrophils, lymphocytes, and platelets associated with teriflunomide use.
L1283: The frequency of cytopenia-related TEAEs, including AESIs, are reported in Table 86. Subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience cytopenia-related TEAEs, particularly lymphopenia and lymphocyte count decreased, which is not unexpected given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy.

L1287: *Reviewer comment: Subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience lymphopenia and lymphocyte count decreased, which is not unexpected given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy. Neutropenia and thrombocytopenia were more common among teriflunomide-treated subjects, which is described in current teriflunomide labeling. However, ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects were more likely to have ANC \le 0.5 \times 10^9/L compared to teriflunomide-treated subjects.*
L1288: ## 8.5.5. Hepatic Disorders
L1289: Hepatic dysfunction was listed as an AESI for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. TEAEs designated as liver-related AESIs by the applicant, as well as liver-related events under the SOCs Hepatobiliary Disorders and Investigations were reviewed. Laboratory results are reviewed in Section 8.4.6.
L1290: Labeling for teriflunomide, the active comparator for these studies, currently contains a boxed warning for hepatotoxicity. Specifically, the boxed warning states that “clinically significant and potentially life-threatening liver injury, including acute liver failure requiring transplant, has been reported in patients treated with AUBAGIO in the postmarketing setting. Concomitant use of AUBAGIO with other hepatotoxic drugs may increase the risk of severe liver injury. Obtain transaminase and bilirubin levels within 6 months prior to initiation of AUBAGIO and monitoring ALT levels at least monthly for 6 months. If drug induced liver injury is suspected, discontinue AUBAGIO and start accelerated elimination procedure.”
L1291: The Drug-Induced Liver Injury (DILI) Team was consulted for review of a reported case of toxic hepatitis and the overall frequency of liver function testing abnormalities observed in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy. Please refer to the Consultation Memorandum by Dr. Paul Hayashi, dated 9/20/2022.
L1292: Four cases meeting Hy's Law laboratory criteria for drug-induced liver injury occurred across Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, 2 of which were in subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy:
L1293: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (Study TG1101-RMS301) receiving ublituximab-xiiy experienced the TEAE hepatitis toxic leading to discontinuation (See Section 8.4.3). This event was determined to be related to acute hepatitis C.
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (Study TG1101-RMS302) receiving ublituximab-xiiy experienced the SAE chronic hepatitis B (See Section 8.4.2)
L1294: *Reviewer comment: The event experienced by Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was determined to be acute hepatitis C (refer to section 8.4.3). This case of hepatitis B is discussed in Section 8.4.2. The hepatic injury is related to hepatitis B in this case, which was likely reactivated due to ublituximab-xiiy, as this is a known safety risk with anti-CD20 therapies.*
L1295: Two additional cases of hepatitis B occurred in Study TG1101-RMS302 (subjects [REDACTED](b) (6) and [REDACTED](b) (6), which are discussed in Section 8.4.2 and 8.4.3, respectively).

L1306: Reviewer comment: Given the known risk of hepatotoxicity risk with teriflunomide, the similar frequency of liver-related adverse events and liver function test elevations observed in the teriflunomide and ublituximab-xiiy groups was concerning. Additionally, the magnitude of LFT elevations appears similar between the treatment groups. However, an underlying mechanism of this potential safety signal is unclear, as monoclonal antibodies are generally not considered to have directly hepatotoxic potential. Please refer to the Consultation Memorandum by Dr. Paul Hayashi, dated 9/20/2022. The DILI team concluded that there did not appear to be a signal for a direct hepatotoxicity (i.e., non-hepatitis B reactivation) risk associated with ublituximab-xiiy.
L1307: ## 8.5.6. Reduction in Immunoglobulins
L1308: Reduction in immunoglobulins (specifically IgG and IgM) is a known potential safety risk with anti-CD20 therapies, especially with long-term use. The applicant designated hypogammaglobulinemia as an AESI for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, and pertinent TEAEs are summarized in Table 90. No cases of hypogammaglobulinemia occurred in these studies, but 3 cases of decreased IgM occurred in the pooled double-blind period of Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302. As discussed in Section 8.4.6, subjects treated with ublituximab-xiiy had a clear decline in IgM levels during the double-blind treatment period for both studies compared to teriflunomide (Figure 13; Figure 22).

This finding is consistent with the observation of IgM decline in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects during double-blind treatment (Section 8.4.6; Figure 13; Figure 22).

L1316: *Reviewer comment: Based upon regulatory experience with other anti-CD20 therapies, the likelihood of observing consistent hypogammaglobulinemia and other immunoglobulin reductions in a 2-year study period is low. However, a trend for declining IgM in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects was observed during the double-blind treatment period. A postmarketing requirement regarding characterization of the long-term risk of decreased IgG and IgM was issued for ocrelizumab and ofatumumab, and will be issued for ublituximab-xiiy, if approved.*
L1317: ## 8.5.7. COVID-19
L1318: The COVID-19 pandemic began in early 2020, with vaccine availability beginning in late 2020 in the United States. Patients receiving immunosuppressive treatments are more likely to
L1319: experience severe disease (e.g., pneumonia) requiring hospitalization. In particular, patients with MS receiving anti-CD20 therapies are thought to be at higher risk of severe COVID-19 disease manifestations, but it is difficult to quantify or compare this risk since no controlled studies of anti-CD20 therapies are ongoing (other than ublituximab-xiiy) over the past few years.
L1320: COVID-19-related TEAEs in the ublituximab-xiiy development program are summarized in Table 92. As indicated in Section 8.5.1, as of 8/13/2022, 24 COVID-19-related deaths have occurred in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects in Studies TG1101-RMS303 and TG1101-RMS201E since the BLA cutoff date. The applicant indicated that 7/24 subjects who died (29%) had received at least one dose of a COVID-19 vaccine while on ublituximab-xiiy, which likely resulted in impaired response to vaccination.

L1324: The applicant provided an updated tabulation of COVID-19 SAEs/TEAEs and deaths following an Information Request sent on 7/28/2022, with response received on 8/18/2022. As of 7/5/2022, in Study TG1101-RMS303, 275 subjects (32.3%) had experienced a COVID-19 infection-related TEAE, including 90 (10.6%) serious infections. In Study TG1101-RMS201E, 9 subjects (20.9%) have experienced a COVID-19 infection-related TEAE, all of which were serious.
L1325: In the Information Request response received 8/18/2022, the applicant provided a review of available information related to COVID-19 epidemiology and severity in patients with RMS, including those on other anti-CD20 therapies. The applicant indicated that the deaths due to COVID-19 in Study TG1101-RMS303 occurred primarily during regional surges in Eastern European countries. The applicant provided data suggesting that patients with RMS who developed COVID-19 were at higher risk for death. The applicant presented results of an analysis suggesting similar rates of COVID-19 mortality among patients on ublituximab-xiiy and
L1326: ocrelizumab. Additionally, the applicant indicated that newer treatment strategies for COVID-19 in the setting of anti-CD20 therapies have helped to mitigate the mortality risk.
L1327: *Reviewer comment: The COVID-19 pandemic began in March of 2020, shortly before the BLA data cutoff. Experience regarding COVID-19 in the ublituximab-xiiy development program has been primarily from the open-label extension studies, and it is therefore difficult to fully address a potentially differential risk of severe disease with ublituximab-xiiy in the absence of controlled data. However, literature suggests an increased risk of severe COVID-19 disease in the setting of other anti-CD20 therapies, which is further complicated by attenuation of vaccine response observed with these therapies. Given the risks of serious infection discussed in Section 8.5.2, serious viral infection, which includes COVID-19, will be discussed in labeling if ublituximab-xiiy is approved.*
L1328: ## 8.5.8. Malignancy
L1329: Malignancy is a known potential safety risk with immunosuppressant treatments, including anti-CD20 therapies, especially with long-term use. The applicant designated malignancy as an AESI for Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, and pertinent TEAEs are summarized in Table 93.

Basal cell carcinoma	0 (0)	0 (0)	0 (0)	0 (0)	1 (2.3)
L1332: Source: ADAE for respective study, as of BLA cutoff date
L1333: Per the 120-Day Safety Update, additional malignancies reported included leukemia (n = 1) and uterine cancer (n = 1), which occurred in subjects who received teriflunomide in the parent study. A case of pseudomyxoma peritonei (verbatim term: "myxoma left sartorius muscle") on [REDACTED](b) (6) was reported in Subject # [REDACTED](b) (6), which occurred during Study TG1101-RMS303. Per the submitted narrative, no treatment was reported and the event was not recovered/not resolved. No action was taken with ublituximab-xiiy for this event.
L1334: *Reviewer comment: Pseudomyxoma peritonei is a rare malignant tumor of the peritoneum. However, the verbatim term "Myxoma left [sartorius] muscle" indicates a benign tumor that is also rare. Given the lack of reported treatment, it is likely that this event was miscoded as pseudomyxoma peritonei.*
L1335: *Reviewer comment: The incidence of malignant neoplasms was low for both treatment groups. No single type of malignancy appears prominent. Additionally the duration of exposure was relatively short and may limit the interpretation of these data.*
L1336: ## 8.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1337: Safety analyses by sex and age group were conducted. Meaningful subpopulation analysis by race was precluded by the small number of non-Caucasian participants enrolled in the clinical studies.
L1338: Review of TEAEs by sex demonstrated that women on ublituximab-xiiy were somewhat more likely to experience upper respiratory tract infections, urinary tract infections/cystitis, and symptoms typically associated with IRRs (e.g., headache, pyrexia, nausea) compared to men (Table 94). Men receiving ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience chills. Otherwise, there did not appear to be any clear differences between men and women, among subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy.
L1339: Review of TEAEs by age (&lt;38 years versus ≥38 years) demonstrated that certain preferred terms associated with respiratory tract infections (e.g., nasopharyngitis, rhinitis, bronchitis, and respiratory tract infection) were somewhat more common in the younger subpopulation receiving ublituximab-xiiy (Table 95). Otherwise, there did not appear to be any clear differences between the age subgroups among subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy.

L1347: ## 8.7. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1348: Not applicable.
L1349: ## 8.8. Additional Safety Explorations
L1350: ## 8.8.1. Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1351: Please refer to Section 8.5.8.
L1352: ## 8.8.2. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1353: The ublituximab-xiiy development program included a small number of pregnancies, with few adverse outcomes. The clinical data are insufficient to support conclusions about effects of ublituximab-xiiy on human reproduction and pregnancy. Please refer to the nonclinical review regarding the reproductive toxicity observed in monkeys.
L1354: In Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, contraception was required throughout the treatment period and for 20 weeks after cessation of active treatment. Pregnancy tests were required every 4 weeks during the double-blind treatment and follow-up periods. See Section 13.2 (Inclusion Criteria).
L1355: In Study TG1101-RMS301, pregnancy was reported in 4 subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy and 1 receiving teriflunomide. In Study TG1101-RMS302, pregnancy was reported in 4 subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy and 1 subject receiving teriflunomide. Two partner pregnancies were reported among subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy. In Study TG1101-RMS201, one subject receiving ublituximab-xiiy became pregnant and 1 pregnancy of a partner was reported.
L1356: Among the 9 subjects who became pregnant while on ublituximab-xiiy, 4 resulted in healthy, full-term infants, 3 resulted in elective abortions, and 2 had unknown outcomes due to withdrawal of consent (one with conception following treatment completion). One subject who underwent elective abortion did so due to ectopic pregnancy, which required surgical intervention that was complicated by salpingitis and death (See Section 8.4.1).
L1357: Among the 3 partner pregnancies in subjects on ublituximab-xiiy, 1 resulted in a healthy, full-term infant, 1 resulted in elective abortion, and 1 had an unknown outcome due to withdrawal of consent.
L1358: ## 8.8.3. Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1359: Not applicable, as subjects in the pediatric age group were not studied.
L1360: ## 8.8.4. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1361: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, and Withdrawal
L1362: There were no cases of overdose. The potential for drug abuse is minimal. No subjects were identified that may have had drug withdrawal symptoms.
L1363: ## Rebound
L1364: Rebound of disease activity is a concern following discontinuation of MS treatments, particularly natalizumab and fingolimod. There was no indication of an increase in relapses after discontinuation of ublituximab-xiiy, although this was not specifically studied.
L1365: ## 8.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1366: ## 8.9.1. Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1367: Not applicable.
L1368: ## 8.9.2. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1369: In the postmarketing setting, expected adverse reactions include infections due to the immunosuppressant effects of ublituximab-xiiy, specifically related to reduction in CD19+ B-cells and immunoglobulins. Given the clinical trial data and regulatory experience with other anti-CD20 therapies, infections could be serious or fatal, and specific infections to note include hepatitis B reactivation, PML, other CNS viral infections, COVID-19, and pneumonia. Hepatitis B reactivation and PML are discussed in labeling for other approved anti-CD20 therapies.
L1370: Infusion-related reactions are also anticipated, but should be at least partially mitigated with required use of premedication and required post-infusion observation.
L1371: Potential safety signals that warrant further evaluation in the postmarketing setting include abnormal liver function testing independent of hepatitis B, reduction in immunoglobulins, immune-mediated colitis, thrombocytopenia, and malignancy.
L1372: ## 8.9.3. Additional Safety Issues From Other Disciplines
L1373: Please refer to discussion of Hepatic Disorders (Section 8.5.5) for interaction with the Drug Induced Liver Injury Team, who were consulted for this review. Please refer to the Consultation Memorandum by Dr. Paul Hayashi, dated 9/20/2022.
L1374: Please refer to the discussion of Human Reproduction and Pregnancy (Section 8.8.2) for interaction with the Division of Maternal and Pediatric Health (DPMH) and Nonclinical
L1375: regarding embryofetal toxicity.

L1377: The primary population for the ISS includes subjects from Studies TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302, comprising a pooled double-blind treatment period population. Adverse events are attributed to the actual treatment (TRT01A).

L1383: ## Summary of Safety Issues
L1384: The risks identified with ublituximab-xiiy are discussed in previous sections of this review, but are summarized below.
L1385: A major risk observed with ublituximab-xiiy is that of infection. Serious and fatal infections occurred in ublituximab-xiiy-treated patients during the controlled trials, and are discussed in Section 8.5.1 and 8.5.2. Additionally, the COVID-19 pandemic that occurred during the Phase 3 trials for ublituximab-xiiy led to observation of several serious and fatal cases of COVID-19 associated with ublituximab-xiiy treatment (Section 8.5.7). However, serious infections also have been reported with other anti-CD20 therapies approved for RMS, and the relative risk compared to teriflunomide appears similar to that observed in controlled trials for ublituximab-xiiy. Ongoing clinical surveillance and monitoring of immunoglobulins may be able to mitigate the risk of more serious infections. In the postmarketing setting, prescribers should also encourage patients to receive all vaccinations, including for COVID-19, prior to initiating treatment with ublituximab-xiiy if possible, given the known interference with vaccine response. These concerns will be discussed in labeling. Additionally, the observed clinical benefit associated with ublituximab-xiiy is thought to outweigh this risk. If ublituximab-xiiy is approved, enhanced pharmacovigilance should be performed for serious and opportunistic infections, including hepatitis B reactivation, PML, and other central nervous system viral infections.
L1386: Infusion-related reactions, including some serious reactions, were observed during ublituximab-xiiy infusion, and are discussed in Section 8.5.3. Subjects enrolled in the clinical trials received premedication for IRR prevention, and in the postmarketing setting, patients will be required to receive premedication with corticosteroids and an antihistamine, and remain under observation post-infusion. Though the risk of IRRs appeared to decrease with subsequent infusions, the applicant provided data indicating that this risk was not negligible. Therefore, post-infusion monitoring will be a requirement for all infusions. The observed clinical benefit associated with ublituximab-xiiy is thought to outweigh this risk.
L1387: Decreased immunoglobulins are a known risk associated with anti-CD20 therapies, particularly with long-term use. Please refer to Section 8.5.6. A decrease in IgM was observed in ublituximab-xiiy-treated subjects during the controlled clinical trials, but in other anti-CD20 therapy development programs, reduction in IgG did not occur until after years of treatment. Therefore, we do not expect to observe decreased IgG in the clinical trial setting. A postmarketing requirement is necessary to better characterize this risk with ublituximab-xiiy and how it translates to risk of infection over time. Additionally, labeling will instruct prescribers to monitor immunoglobulins during treatment with ublituximab-xiiy.
L1388: Cytopenias were considered an AESI by the applicant, and are discussed in Section 8.5.4. Lymphopenia is not unexpected given the mechanism of action of ublituximab-xiiy, but neutropenia was observed at a rate similar to that of teriflunomide, which is labeled for neutropenia. Additionally, subjects receiving ublituximab-xiiy were more likely to experience ANC &lt;0.5 \times 10^9/L compared to those on teriflunomide. However, there was no obvious association of neutropenia with infection.
L1389: Embryofetal toxicity is discussed in the nonclinical and DPMH reviews, and will be addressed in labeling. The nonclinical embryofetal safety results are concerning for a risk in addition to that associated with B-cell depletion. The clinical data obtained during the RMS development program are insufficient to support conclusions about effects of ublituximab-xiiy on human reproduction and pregnancy.
L1390: Other potential signals that warrant ongoing and potentially enhanced pharmacovigilance in the postmarketing setting include pancreatitis (given the observation of elevated pancreatic enzymes), hepatic disorders (independent of Hepatitis B reactivation), thrombocytopenia, immune-mediated colitis (given recent identification of this risk with ocrelizumab) and malignancy.
L1391: Overall, both the benefit and risk of treatment of RMS with ublituximab-xiiy appears consistent with that of other anti-CD20 therapies approved for RMS.

L1408: ## 13.2. Study TG1101-RMS301 and TG1101-RMS302 Eligibility Criteria
L1409: ## Inclusion Criteria:
L1410: Subjects must meet the following inclusion criteria to be eligible for participation in this study:
L1411: - 18-55 age
- Diagnosis of RMS (McDonald criteria 2010; Appendix C)
- ≥ 2 relapses in prior 2 years or 1 relapse in the year prior to screening and/or ≥1 Gd enhancing lesion
- Documented MRI of brain with abnormalities consistent with MS
- Active disease
- EDSS 0-5.5 (inclusive) at screening
L1412: - B cell counts \ge 5\% of total lymphocytes
- Neurologic stability \ge 30 days prior to screening and baseline
- Female subjects who are not of child-bearing potential, have documented surgical sterilization (see Appendix A), and female subjects of child-bearing potential who have a negative serum pregnancy test at baseline. Female subjects of child-bearing potential (see Appendix A), and all male partners must consent to use a medically/clinically acceptable method of contraception throughout the treatment period and for 20 weeks after the cessation of active treatment. Female subjects of child-bearing potential (see Appendix A) must agree to undertake urine pregnancy tests every 4 weeks during active treatment and the follow up period.
- Fertile male subjects participating in the study who are sexually active with women of childbearing potential, must agree to use a condom during the treatment period and for an additional 20 weeks after cessation of active treatment. Agree to use an accelerated elimination procedure after the last dose of study medications or early termination from the study (see Section 6.10.1)
- Willingness and ability to comply with trial and follow-up procedures, give written consent
L1413: ## Exclusion Criteria:
L1414: Subjects who meet any of the following exclusion criteria are not to be enrolled to this study:
L1415: - Treatment with Anti-CD20 or other B cell directed treatment
- Treatment with the following therapies at any time prior to randomization:Alemtuzumab
- Natalizumab,
- Teriflunomide,
- Leflunomide, and
- Stem cell transplantation
- Contraindications to teriflunomide or incompatibility with use of teriflunomide.
- Therapies that are disallowed (minimum of 4 weeks prior to randomization): phenytoin, warfarin, tolbutamide, St John's Wort or cholestyramine
- Prior DMT exposure within months of screening:24 months with cladribine
- 6 months with daclizumab, azathioprine, methotrexate, or cyclophosphamide
- 90 days with fingolimod, or experimental S1P modulators, IV immunoglobulin, and plasmapheresis
- 30 days with glatiramer acetate, interferons, dimethyl fumarate, laquinimod or glucocorticoids
- Diagnosed with Primary Progressive MS (PPMS)
- Pregnant or nursing
- \ge 10 years disease duration from onset with subjects EDSS \le 2.0
L1416: - Contraindication for MRI and/or gadolinium
- Known presence of other neurologic disorders that may mimic MS
- Current evidence or known history of clinically significant infection including:Chronic or ongoing active viral, bacterial, or fungal infectious disease requiring long term systemic treatment such as, but not limited to: PML, chronic renal infection, chronic chest infection with bronchiectasis, tuberculosis (TB), or active hepatitis C
- Previous serious opportunistic or atypical infections
- History of positive serology for hepatitis B or hepatitis C or HIV
- History of clinically significant CNS trauma (e.g., traumatic brain injury, cerebral contusion, spinal cord compression)
- History of liver disease, including but not limited to:Known history of active hepatitis B or C any time prior to randomization or known history of active hepatitis A within 3 years prior to randomization
- Presence of clinically significant chronic liver or biliary disease
- Moderate or severe hepatic impairment defined as Child Pugh Score B or C, respectively, based on measurement of total bilirubin, serum albumin, International Normalized Ratio (INR) and as well as on presence /absence and severity of ascites and hepatic encephalopathy
- Any of the following abnormal laboratory values at screening or first infusion:ALT/SGPT&gt;2X the Upper Limit of Normal (ULN)
- AST/SGOT&gt;2X ULN
- Previous diagnosis with a congenital or acquired immunodeficiency (AIDS)
- History of renal impairment, including, but not limited to:Hypoproteinemia (e.g., in case of severe renal disease or nephrotic syndrome) with serum albumin &lt;3.0 g/dL
- Severe renal insufficiency requiring renal dialysis
- Past or current history of medically significant adverse effects (including allergic reactions) from:Corticosteroids
- Diphenhydramine
- Murine or mouse/human chimeric antibodies
- Subjects with significantly impaired bone marrow function or significant anemia, leukopenia, or thrombocytopeniaHematocrit &lt;24% and/or
- Absolute white blood cell count &lt;4,000 cells/mm3 and/or
- Platelet count &lt;150,000 cells/mm3 and/or
- Absolute neutrophil \le 1,500 cells/mm3
- Absolute lymphocyte counts less than 1000/microliter
- Any severe and/or uncontrolled medical conditions or other conditions that could affect their participation in the study such as:
L1417: - Symptomatic, or history of documented congestive heart failure (New York Heart Association functional classification III-IV [see Appendix B]
- QTcF: Female &gt;450 msec; male &gt; 430 msec Angina not well-controlled by medication
- Poorly controlled or clinically significant atherosclerotic vascular disease including cerebrovascular accident (CVA), transient ischemic attack (TIA), angioplasty, cardiac or vascular stenting in the past 6 months prior to screening
20. Other significant concurrent, uncontrolled medical condition including, but not limited to, cardiac, renal, hepatic, hematological, gastrointestinal, endocrine, immunodeficiency syndrome, pulmonary, cerebral, psychiatric, or neurological disease which could affect the subject's safety, impair the subject's reliable participation in the trial, impair the evaluation of endpoints, or necessitate the use of medication not allowed by the protocol, as determined by the PI of the trial

21. Current participation in any other interventional clinical trial. Participation in non-interventional trial requires approval by the Sponsor

22. Inability or unwillingness to comply with study and/or follow-up procedures outlined in the protocol

23. Lack of immunity to varicella as determined by screening based on the level of VZV IgG. Subject may receive vaccine and be rescreened

24. Vaccination with live virus within 2 months of randomization

25. History or presence of malignancy (except for surgically excised basal or squamous cell skin lesions), lymphoproliferative disease, or history of total lymphoid irradiation or bone marrow transplantation

Infusion (Vial icon)

Teriflunomide Arm: Administered orally (blue arrows) starting at Week 4, continuing until the last day of Week 95. Clinical assessments (MRI, B cell & Labs) are performed at Weeks 0, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72, 84, 96, 100, 104, 108, 112, and 116.

The study includes a 4-week screening period, followed by the Treatment Period (Weeks 4 to 95 for Teriflunomide, Weeks 4 to 116 for Ublituximab Infusions), and a Follow-up Period (Weeks 96 to 116).
L1433: Line graph showing Mean Percentage Change in Brain Volume from Baseline versus Study Week (Baseline, Week 24, Week 48, Week 96) for two treatment groups: Ublituximab (solid blue line) and Teriflunomide (dashed red line). The Teriflunomide group shows a slight increase to approximately +0.1% at Week 24, then declines, reaching approximately -0.4% at Week 96. Error bars (95% Wald Confidence Intervals) are displayed for all data points.

The Teriflunomide group shows a slight increase to approximately 0.05% at Week 24, followed by a decline, reaching approximately -0.55% at Week 96. Error bars representing 95% Wald Confidence Intervals are displayed for all data points.

L1438: Figure 7 is a line graph showing the Median Absolute Neutrophil Count (\times 10^9/L) over time for two treatment groups: Ublituximab/xiiy (Oral Placebo) and Teriflunomide (IV Placebo). The X-axis represents Time (Baseline, Week 1 Day 2, Week 2, Week 3, Week 4, Week 8, Week 12, Week 16, Week 20, Week 24, Week 36, Week 48, Week 60, Week 72, Week 84, Week 96, Week 100, Week 104, Week 116). The Teriflunomide group (blue line) shows a lower peak at Week 1 Day 2 (around 9.5 \times 10^9/L), followed by a gradual decline, stabilizing around 3.5 \times 10^9/L by Week 3. Both groups show similar ANC levels from Week 3 onwards, with the Teriflunomide group generally maintaining slightly lower ANC values. After Week 96 (when Teriflunomide was stopped), the ANC in the Teriflunomide group begins to recover, rising towards the level of the Ublituximab/xiiy group by Week 116.
